De Giorgi Argument for non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with soft potentials
Chuqi Cao

Abstract
. In this paper, we consider the global well-posedness to the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with soft potential in the L ∞ L^{\infty} setting. We show that when the initial data is close to equilibrium and the perturbation is small in L 2 ∩ L ∞ L^{2}\cap L^{\infty} polynomial weighted space, the Boltzmann equation has a global solution in the weighted L 2 ∩ L ∞ L^{2}\cap L^{\infty} space. The ingredients of the proof lie in strong averaging lemma, new polynomial weighted estimate for the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation and the L 2 L^{2} level set Di Giorgi iteration method developed in [ 9 ] . The convergence to the equilibrium state in both L 2 L^{2} and L ∞ L^{\infty} spaces is also proved.



1.
Introduction

The Boltzmann equation reads

(1)

\partial_{t}F+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F=Q(F,F),\quad F(0,x,v)=F_{0}(x,v),

where
F(t,x,v)\geq 0
is a distributional functions of colliding particles which, at time
t>0
and position
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3}
, move with velocity
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. We remark that the Boltzmann equation is one of the fundamental equations of mathematical physics and is a cornerstone of statistical physics. The Boltzmann collision operator
Q
is a bilinear operator which acts only on the velocity variable
v
, that is

Q(G,F)(v)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(G^{\prime}_{*}F^{\prime}-G_{*}F)d\sigma dv_{*}.

Let us give some explanations on the collision operator.

(1)

We use the standard shorthand
F=F(v),G_{*}=G(v_{*}),F^{\prime}=F(v^{\prime}),G^{\prime}_{*}=G(v^{\prime}_{*})
, where
v^{\prime},v^{\prime}_{*}
are given by

v^{\prime}=\frac{v+v_{*}}{2}+\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{2}\sigma,\quad v_{*}^{\prime}=\frac{v+v_{*}}{2}-\frac{|v-v_{*}|}{2}\sigma,\quad\sigma\in\mathbb{S}^{2}.

This representation follows the parametrization of set of solutions of the physical law of elastic collision:

v+v_{*}=v^{\prime}+v^{\prime}_{*},\quad|v|^{2}+|v_{*}|^{2}=|v^{\prime}|^{2}+|v^{\prime}_{*}|^{2}.

(2)

The nonnegative function
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)
in the collision operator is called the Boltzmann collision kernel. It is always assumed to depend only on
|v-v_{*}|
and the deviation angle
\theta
through
\cos\theta:=\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma
.

(3)

In the present work, our
basic assumptions on the kernel
B
can be concluded as follows:

\mathbf{(A1).}

The Boltzmann kernel
B
takes the form:
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma)
, where
b
is a nonnegative function.

\mathbf{(A2).}

The angular function
b(\cos\theta)
is not locally integrable and it satisfies

\mathcal{K}\theta^{-1-2s}\leq\sin\theta b(\cos\theta)\leq\mathcal{K}^{-1}\theta^{-1-2s},~\mbox{with}~0<s<1,~\mathcal{K}>0.

\mathbf{(A3).}

The parameter
\gamma
and
s
satisfy the condition
-3<\gamma\leq 0,s\in(0,1)
and
\gamma+2s>-1.

\mathbf{(A4).}

Without lose of generality, we may assume that
B(v-v_{*},\sigma)
is supported in the set
0\leq\theta\leq\pi/2
, i.e.
\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma\geq 0
, for otherwise
B
can be replaced by its symmetrized form:

\overline{B}(v-v_{*},\sigma)=|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\big(b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma)+b(\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot(-\sigma))\big)\mathrm{1}_{\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}\cdot\sigma\geq 0},

where
\mathrm{1}_{A}
is the characteristic function of the set
A
.

Remark 1.1
.

For inverse repulsive potential, it holds that
\gamma=\frac{p-5}{p-1}
and
s=\frac{1}{p-1}
with
p>2
. It is easy to check that
\gamma+4s=1
which means that assumption
\gamma+2s>-1
is satisfied for the full range of the inverse power law model. Generally, the case
\gamma>0
,
\gamma=0
, and
\gamma<0
correspond to so-called hard, Maxwellian, and soft potentials respectively. Assumption
\mathbf{(A3)}
corresponds to soft potential and Maxwellian molecule case.

1.1.
Basic properties and the perturbation equation

We recall some basic facts on the Boltzmann equation.

\bullet

Conservation Law.
Formally if
F
is the solution to the Boltzmann equation (
1
) with the initial data
F_{0}
, then it enjoys the conservation of mass, momentum and the energy, that is,

(2)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}F(t,x,v)\varphi(v)dvdx=0,\quad\varphi(v)=1,v,|v|^{2}.

For simplicity, we introduce the normalization identities on the initial data
F_{0}
which satisfies

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,dvdx=1,\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,v\,dvdx=0,\quad\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{0}(x,v)\,|v|^{2}\,dvdx=3.

This means that the equilibrium associated to (
1
) will be the standard Gaussian function, i.e.

\mu(v):=(2\pi)^{-3/2}e^{-|v|^{2}/2},

which enjoys the same mass, momentum and energy as
F_{0}
.

\bullet

Perturbation Equation.
In the perturbation framework, let
f
be the perturbation such that

F=\mu+f.

The Boltzmann equation (
1
) becomes

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=Q(\mu,f)+Q(f,\mu)+Q(f,f):=Lf+Q(f,f)

with the linearized operator is defined by
L:=Q(\mu,\cdot)+Q(\cdot,\mu)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}f
.

1.2.
Brief review of previous results

In what follows we recall some known results on the Landau and Boltzmann equations with a focus on the topics under consideration in this paper, particularly on global existence and large-time behavior of solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous equations in the perturbation framework. For global solutions to the renormalized equation with large initial data, we mention the classical works
[
18
,
19
,
51
,
60
,
61
,
25
,
6
]
. We mention
[
16
,
17
]
for the best regularity results available for the Boltzmann equation without cut-off. For the stability of vacuum, see
[
52
,
34
,
15
]
for the Landau, cutoff and non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with moderate soft potential respectively.

In the near Maxwellian framework, global existence and large-time behavior of solutions to the spatially inhomogeneous equations is proved in
[
31
,
32
,
56
,
57
]
for the cutoff Boltzmann equation and in
[
30
]
for the Landau equation. For the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation it is first proved in
[
27
,
28
,
2
,
3
,
4
,
5
]
, see also
[
23
]
for a recent work on such topic.
We also refer to
[
35
,
36
,
37
,
22
,
62
,
21
,
24
]
for the former works on the Vlasov-Poisson/Maxwell-Boltzmann/Landau equation near Maxwellian. We remark here all these works above are base on the following decomposition

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=L_{\mu}f+\Gamma(f,f),\quad L_{\mu}f=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}(Q(\mu,\sqrt{\mu}f)+Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\mu)),\quad\Gamma(f,f)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}Q(\sqrt{\mu}f,\sqrt{\mu}f),

which means the result are in
\mu^{-1/2}
weighted space.

For the inhomogeneous equations with polynomial weight perturbation near Maxwellian, in Gualdani-Mischler-Mouhot
[
29
]
the authors first prove the global existence and large time behavior of solutions with polynomial velocity weight for the cutoff Boltzmann equation with hard potential. This method is generalized to the Landau equation in
[
13
,
14
]
and the cutoff Boltzmann with soft potential in
[
10
]
. The non-cutoff Boltzmann equation with hard potential is proved in
[
40
,
8
]
, and the soft potential case is proved in
[
11
]
.

For the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation,
[
43
,
44
,
45
,
46
,
47
,
53
]
obtains global regularity and long time behavior by assuming a uniformly bound in
t,x
such that

0<m_{0}\leq M(t,x)\leq M_{0},\quad E(t,x)\leq E_{0},\quad H(t,x)\leq H_{0},

for some constant
m_{0},M_{0},E_{0},H_{0}
, where

M(t,x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}f(t,x,v)dv,\quad E(t,x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}f(t,x,v)|v|^{2}dv,\quad H(t,x)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}f(t,x,v)\ln f(t,x,v)dv.

For the Landau equation the local Hölder estimate is proved in
[
26
]
for and higher regularity of solutions is studied in
[
42
]
by applying a kinetic version of Schauder estimates. These papers can be seen as under conditional regularity. More specifically, solutions with properties which remain to be justified in general are shown to have quantitative regularization. In this respect, these works are mainly concerned with the regularization mechanism of kinetic equations while our goal is to establish a self-contained well-posedness result.

In the near Maxwellian framework, former mentioned works mainly focus on the
L^{2}
well-posedness, there are also several results for
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness result near Maxwellian. For the cutoff Boltzmann equation near equilibrium, the
L^{2}-L^{\infty}
approach has been introduced in
[
58
,
33
]
and apply to various contexts, see
[
49
,
38
]
and the reference therein for example. Also see
[
20
]
for the solution with large amplitude initial data under the assumption of small entropy. For the Landau equation, in
[
48
]
the authors proved a global
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness result near Maxwellian by using strategies inspired by
[
26
]
. However, it is not clear to us how to extend the argument in
[
48
]
to the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation since the Landau operator is closer to classical nonlinear parabolic operators. For the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation,
[
9
]
proves a
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness result for the hard potential
\gamma>0
by using Di Giorgi iteration developed in
[
7
]
. Another
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
well-posedness result is obtained in
[
54
]
by using the result in
[
45
,
46
,
47
]
for moderate soft potential
\gamma+2s\geq 0
. In this paper we will use the Di Giorgi iteration method developed in
[
7
,
9
]
together with polynomial weighted estimate for the non-cutoff Boltzmann equation developed in
[
11
]
to prove the well-posedness for the case
-3<\gamma\leq 0
.

Recently in
[
41
]
, the authors proved independently the global well-posedness
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
near Maxwellian by using the result in
[
45
,
46
,
47
]
, but their proof requires the lower bound assumption on the initial data

f_{0}(x,v)\geq\delta\quad\hbox{for}\quad x\in B_{r}(x,v)

for some constant
(x,v)
and
\delta,r>0
. And in our paper we also obtained the rate of convergence to the equilibrium.

1.3.
Main results and notations

Let us first introduce the function spaces and notations.

\bullet
For any
p\in[1,+\infty)
,
q\in{\mathbb{R}}
the
L^{p}_{q}
norm is defined by

\|f\|_{L^{p}_{q}}^{p}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f(v)|^{p}\langle v\rangle^{pq}dv,

where the Japanese bracket
\langle v\rangle
is defined as
\langle v\rangle:=(1+|v|^{2})^{1/2}
.

\bullet
For real numbers
m,l
, we define the weighted Sobolev space
H_{l}^{m}
by

H^{m}_{l}:=\{f(v)||f|_{H^{m}_{l}}=|\langle\cdot\rangle^{l}\langle D\rangle^{m}f|_{L^{2}}<+\infty\},

where
a(D)
is a pseudo-differential operator with the symbol
a(\xi)
and it is defined as

(a(D)f)(v):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}e^{i(v-u)\xi}a(\xi)f(u)dud\xi.

and we denote
H^{m}:=H^{m}_{0}
.

\bullet
For function
f(x,v),x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{3},v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, the norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{\alpha}_{x}H^{m}_{l}}
is defined as

\|f\|_{H^{\alpha}_{x}H^{m}_{l}}:=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle D_{x}\rangle^{\alpha}f(x,\cdot)\|^{2}_{H^{m}_{l}}dx\right)^{1/2}.

If
\alpha=0
,
H^{0}_{x}H^{m}_{l}=L^{2}_{x}H^{m}_{l}
.

\bullet
The
L\log L
space is defined as

L\log L:=\Big\{f(v)|\|f\|_{L\log L}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f|\log(1+|f|)dv\Big\}.

The
L\log L
norm is defined by

\|f\|_{L\log L}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f|\log(|f|+1)dv.

\bullet
We write
a\lesssim b
indicate that there is a uniform constant
C
, which may be different on different lines, such that
a\leq Cb
. We use the notation
a\sim b
whenever
a\lesssim b
and
b\lesssim a
. We denote
C_{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}}
by a constant depending on parameters
a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{n}
. Moreover, we use parameter
\epsilon
to represent different positive numbers much less than 1 and determined in different cases.

\bullet
We use
(f,g)
to denote the inner product of
f,g
in the
v
variable
(f,g)_{L^{2}_{v}}
for short and we use
(f,g)_{L^{2}_{k}}
to denote
(f,g\langle v\rangle^{2k})
.

\bullet
For any function
f
we define

\|f(\theta)\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}:=\int_{\lx@sectionsign^{2}}f(\theta)d\sigma=2\pi\int_{0}^{\pi}f(\theta)\sin\theta d\theta.

\bullet
For
p\in[1,\infty)
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
we define the Bessel potential space as

H^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}):=\{f(v)||\langle D\rangle^{\beta}f|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}<+\infty\},

and the associated norm is defined by

\|u\|_{H^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}:=\|\langle D\rangle^{\beta}u\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

\bullet
For any
p\in[1.\infty),\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, Sobolev-Slobodeckij space is defined as

W^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}):=\left\{f\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\beta p}}dxdy\right\},

and the associated norm is defined by

\|u\|_{W^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}:=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|f(x)|^{p}dx+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{d+\beta p}}dxdy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

The Bessel potential space and Sobolev-Slobodeckij space agree for
p=2
. More generally, the following condition holds:

(i)For all
p\in(1,2]
,
\beta\in(0,1)
it holds that

\|u\|_{H^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|u\|_{W^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

(ii)For all
p\in[2,+\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,1)
it holds that

\|u\|_{W^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C\|u\|_{H^{\beta,p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}.

The proof can be found in
[
55
]
, Chapter V.

\bullet
For the linearized operator
L
we have

\ker(L)=\text{span}\{\mu,v_{1}\mu,v_{2}\mu,v_{3}\mu,|v|^{2}\mu\}.

We define the projection onto
\ker(L)
by

(3)

Pf:=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}fdvdx\right)\mu+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}v_{i}fdvdx\right)v_{i}\mu+\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{\sqrt{6}}fdvdx\right)\frac{|v|^{2}-3}{\sqrt{6}}\mu.

For any space
X
, define the subspace
\Pi X
by

(4)

\Pi X:=\{f\in X|Pf=0\}.

\bullet
For any
k\in{\mathbb{R}},\gamma\in(-3,1]
, we define

\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\mu(v_{*})|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdv_{*}.

It is easily seen that
\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2,*}}\sim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}
.

\bullet
For any
l\geq 0,K\geq 0
, define the level set function

(5)

f_{K}^{l}:=f\langle v\rangle^{l}-K,\quad f_{K,+}^{l}:=f_{K}^{l}1_{\{f_{K}^{l}\geq 0\}},\quad f_{K,-}^{l}:=f_{K}^{l}1_{\{f_{K}^{l}<0\}}.

\bullet
. For some
\alpha\geq 0
, we introduce a regularizing linear operator defined by

(6)

L_{\alpha}\phi(v)=-(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\phi-\nabla_{v}\cdot(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}\phi)),\quad\alpha\geq 0.

\bullet
In the whole paper, we define the cutoff function
\chi\in C^{\infty}
satisfies
0\leq\chi\leq 1
,
|\nabla\chi|\leq 4/\delta_{0}
and

(7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\chi}(x):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad|x|\leq\delta_{0}\\
&0,\quad|x|\geq 2\delta_{0},\end{aligned}\right.

for some small constant
\delta_{0}>0
.

1.4.
Main results

We may now state our main results.

Theorem 1.2
.

Assume that
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
, for any smooth function
f
, consider the Cauchy problem

\partial_{t}f=Lf+Q(f,f),\quad\mu+f\geq 0,\quad f(0)=f_{0},\quad Pf_{0}=0.

Suppose the kernel
B
verifying
(
\mathbf{(A1)}
-
\mathbf{(A4)}
)
. Then for any
k_{0}\geq 14
large, there exist
k>k_{0}\geq 14
large and
\epsilon>0
small such that if

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\epsilon,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,

then there exist a nonnegative solution
F=\mu+f\geq 0
,
F\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
to the Boltzmann equation (
1
). Moreover, if
\gamma=0
, we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda t},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda_{1}t},

for some constant
\delta_{1},C,\lambda,\lambda_{1}>0
. If
\gamma<0
, for any
14\leq k_{1}<k
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{1}}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C(1+t)^{-\frac{k-k_{1}}{|\gamma|}},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f(t)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C(1+t)^{-\alpha_{1}},

for some constant
C,\delta_{1},\alpha>0
.

Remark 1.3
.

We only focus on the Maxwellian molecule and soft potential case
-3<\gamma\leq 0
since the hard potential case is already proved in
[
9
]
.

\bullet

Comment on the solutions.

The spectral gap for the polynomial weight
\langle v\rangle^{k}
is
\gamma\geq 0
instead of
\gamma+2s\geq 0
. which is proved in
[
11
]
. In fact it is proved in
[
11
]
such that the spectral gap for
m=e^{k\langle v\rangle^{\beta}},k>0,\beta\in(0,2]
is
\gamma+\beta s\geq 0
.

1.5.
Strategies and ideas of the proof

In this subsection, we will explain main strategies and ideas of the proof for our results. First observe the fact that if we assume the smallness of

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\leq\delta_{0},

for some
\delta_{0}>0
small, then we can obtain
L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}
energy estimates with general weights. Using classical velocity averaging lemma we can transfer the regularity from the velocity to the spatial variable to obtain regularization in the spatial variable
H^{s^{\prime}}_{x}L^{2}_{x}
for any
s^{\prime}\in(0.\frac{s}{2(s+3)})
. This Hypoellipticity allows us to apply the De Giorgi argument through embeddings of Sobolev spaces to
L^{p}
spaces.

Since the time averaging lemma requires
p>1
, when applying the time averaging lemma to
(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}
, we need the estimate of
\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}
, we take
p
very close to 1 such that the
L^{2p}
norm can be estimated by
H^{s^{\prime}}_{x}L^{2}_{x}
and
L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}
norm using Sobolev embedding.

More precisely, we construct the following energy functional:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(K,T_{1},T_{2}):=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dxd\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{1/p}.

The main step in the De Giorgi argument is to prove that there exist a constant
K_{0}\geq 0
such that

M_{k}:=K_{0}(1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}),\quad\mathcal{E}_{k}:=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k},0,T),\quad\mathcal{E}_{k}\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}.

Using Di Giorgi iteration we can prove there exist a constant
K_{0}^{\prime}
such that if
K_{0}\geq K_{0}^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{E}_{k}\to 0
, which implies

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f_{K_{0},+}^{l}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=0.

similar results is proved for
f_{-}
. Thus instead of directly proving
\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta
for some
\delta>0
, we prove

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f_{K_{0},+}^{l}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=0,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f_{K_{0},-}^{l}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=0,

for some constant
K_{0}>0
, which could implies

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq K_{0},

See also Figure 1 in
[
9
]
for a better understanding for the structure of the proof.

The strategy described above is applied first to the linearized equation and then to the nonlinear equation to obtain local solutions with
L^{\infty}
-bounds to the original Boltzmann equation. Although obtaining a solution to the linearized equation is fairly straight-forward, significant effort has been carried out to show the
L^{\infty}
-bounds of the solution. More precisely, we first prove the local existence of solutions for the linearized equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),u+f),

where
L_{\alpha}
is a regularizing term defined in (
6
) and
\chi
is the cutoff function defined in (
7
). Using fixed point theorem we obtained a local solution for the nonlinear equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f),\mu+f),

then we prove that the solution satisfies

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0},

hence the solution becomes a solution to

(8)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+f,\mu+f).

Finally we prove that the priori estimates of (
8
) are independent of
\epsilon
, thus we can pass the limit in
\epsilon
to prove the local existence of the Boltzmann equation (
1
). Finally, combining the local existence with the global estimate obtained in
[
11
]
we obtain a global solution to the original Boltzmann equation.

1.6.
Organization of the paper

After introducing a technical toolbox in Section
2
, Section
3
is devoted to the upper bounds and coercivity estimate on collision operator
Q
. In Section
4
we establish the estimates for the
L^{2}
level set function
f_{K,+}^{l}
and we establish the
L^{\infty}
bound for the linearized equation. These estimates are used in Section
5
to show the existence of solution to the linear equation. In Section
6
we establish the nonlinear counterparts of the estimates of those in Section
3
and Section
4
, then apply them to establish the local well-posedness of the Boltzmann equation. In Section
7
we combine the results in Section
6
and global estimate of the Boltzmann operator to establish the global well-posedness of the nonlinear Boltzmann equation. The well-posedness proved in Section
7
is only for weak singularity kernels, we extend the result to the strong singularity case in Section
8
.

2.
Preliminaries

In this section we recall several lemmas which is useful in later proof, some of them may be elementary but we still write it for completeness.

Lemma 2.1
.

(
[
1
]
) For any smooth function
f,g,b
, we have

(1) (Regular change of variables)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v^{\prime})d\sigma dv=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{1}{\cos^{3+\gamma}(\theta/2)}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v)d\sigma dv.

(2) (Singular change of variables)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v^{\prime})d\sigma dv_{*}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{1}{\sin^{3+\gamma}(\theta/2)}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f(v_{*})d\sigma dv_{*}.

Lemma 2.2
.

For any smooth function
f,g,h,b
, for any
\gamma\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}gh^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\right)^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}|^{2}|g|dvdv_{*}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}|g||h^{\prime}|^{2}dvdv^{\prime}.

and

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}gh^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{-\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\right)^{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}||g|^{2}dvdv_{*}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v_{*}-v^{\prime}|^{\gamma}|f_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}dv_{*}dv^{\prime}.

Proof.

The proof is the combination of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and regular/singular change of variable.
∎

Then we recall the upper and lower bound for the Boltzmann operator

Lemma 2.3
.

(
[
39
]
Theorem 1.1)
Let
w_{1},w_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
a,b\in[0,2s]
with
w_{1}+w_{2}=\gamma+2s
and
a+b=2s
. Then for any smooth functions
g,h,f
we have

(1) if
\gamma+2s>0
we have

|(Q(g,h),f)_{L^{2}_{v}}|\lesssim\|(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+2s+(-w_{1})^{+}(-w_{2})^{+}}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}})\|h\|_{H^{a}_{w_{1}}}\|f\|_{H^{b}_{w_{2}}}.

(2) if
\gamma+2s=0
we have

|(Q(g,h),f)_{L^{2}_{v}}|\lesssim(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{w_{3}}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}})\|h\|_{H^{a}_{w_{1}}}\|f\|_{H^{b}_{w_{2}}},

where
w_{3}=\max\{\delta,(-w_{1})^{+}+(-w_{2})^{+}\}
, with
\delta>0
sufficiently small.

(3) if
\gamma+2s<0
we have

|(Q(g,h),f)_{L^{2}_{v}}|\lesssim(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{w_{4}}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{-(\gamma+2s)}})\|h\|_{H^{a}_{w_{1}}}\|f\|_{H^{b}_{w_{2}}},

where
w_{3}=\max\{-(\gamma+2s),\gamma+2s+(-w_{1})^{+}+(-w_{2})^{+}\}
.

Lemma 2.4
.

(
[
39
]
, Theorem 1.2)
Suppose that
g
is a non-negative and smooth function verifying that

\|g\|_{L^{1}}\geq\delta,\quad\|g\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|g\|_{L\log L}<\lambda,

Then there exist
C_{1}(\lambda,\delta)
and
C_{2}(\lambda,\delta)
such that

(1) If
\gamma+2s\geq 0
, then we have

(-Q(g,f),f)_{L^{2}_{v}}\geq C_{1}(\lambda,\delta)\|f\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}-C_{2}(\lambda,\delta)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}.

(2) If
-1-2s<\gamma<-2s

(-Q(g,f),f)_{L^{2}_{v}}\geq C_{1}(\lambda,\delta)\|f\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}-C_{2}(\lambda,\delta)(1+\|g\|_{L^{p}_{|\gamma|}}^{\frac{(\gamma+2s+3)p}{(\gamma+2s+3)p-3}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2},

with
p>\frac{3}{3+\gamma+2s}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

(Cancellation Lemma) (
[
1
]
, Lemma 1) For any smooth function
f
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}B(v-v_{*},\sigma)(f^{\prime}-f)dvd\sigma=(f*S)(v_{*}),

where

S(z)=|\mathbb{S}^{1}|\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\sin\theta\left[\frac{1}{\cos^{3}(\theta/2)}B\left(\frac{|z|}{\cos(\theta/2)},\cos\theta\right)-B(|z|,cos\theta)\right].

Lemma 2.6
.

(
[
59
]
, Section 1.4)(Pre-post collisional change of variable) For any function
F
smooth enough we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F(v,v_{*},v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime})B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)dvdv_{*}d\sigma=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}F(v^{\prime},v_{*}^{\prime},v,v_{*})B(|v-v_{*}|,\cos\theta)dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

Lemma 2.7
.

(Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality) (
[
50
]
, Chapter 4)
Let
p,r>1
and
0<\lambda<N
with
1/p+\lambda/n+1/r=2
. Let
f\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
h\in L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{N})
. Then there exist a constant
C(n,\lambda,p)
, independent of
f
and
h
, such that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{N}}f(x)|x-y|^{-\lambda}h(y)dxdy\leq C(n,\lambda,p)\|f\|_{L^{p}}\|h\|_{L^{r}}.

In this paper we will use the following representation of
v^{\prime}
which can be proved directly. We have

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2}=\langle v\rangle^{2}\cos^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}+2\cos\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|v\cdot\omega,\quad\omega\perp(v-v_{*}),\quad v\cdot w=v_{*}\cdot w,

where
\omega=\frac{\sigma-(\sigma\cdot k)k}{|\sigma-(\sigma\cdot k)k|}
with
k=\frac{v-v_{*}}{|v-v_{*}|}
. We can further decompose
\omega
by

(9)

\omega=\tilde{\omega}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{v^{\prime}-v_{*}}{|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|}\sin\frac{\theta}{2},\quad\tilde{\omega}=\frac{v^{\prime}-v}{|v^{\prime}-v|},\quad\tilde{\omega}\perp(v^{\prime}-v_{*}).

For the
\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}
we have

Lemma 2.8
.

(
[
10
]
, Lemma 2.7)
For any constant
k\geq 4
we have

\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{k}-\langle v\rangle^{k}\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}=k\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\cos^{k-1}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|(v_{*}\cdot\omega)+L_{1}+L_{2},

with

|L_{1}|\leq C_{k}\sin^{k-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{k}\langle v\rangle^{2},\quad|L_{2}|\leq C_{k}\langle v\rangle^{k-2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2},

for some constant
C_{k}>0
.

By symmetry we have

Lemma 2.9
.

For smooth function
f,g
and for any constant
k
, we have

\Gamma:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}f_{*}g^{\prime}dvdv_{*}d\sigma=0.

Proof.

By the regular change of variable
v\to v^{\prime}
and take the new
v^{\prime}-v_{*}
as the north pole, recall
\tilde{\omega}\perp(v^{\prime}-v_{*})
. Then we have

\Gamma=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{1}{\cos^{4+\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}f_{*}g^{\prime}dv^{\prime}dv_{*}\sin\theta d\theta d\phi,

where
\tilde{\omega}=(\cos\phi,\sin\phi,0)
. It’s easily seen that the integration in
\phi
gives that
\Gamma=0
.
∎

We introduce some
L^{p}
inequalities related to the singularity of the kernel.

Lemma 2.10
.

(
[
11
]
, Lemma 2.6)
Suppose
\gamma\in(-3,1],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. For any smooth function
g
and
f
we have

\mathcal{R}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}f^{2}dv_{*}dv\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}.

Lemma 2.11
.

(
[
10
]
, Lemma 2.5) Suppose
\gamma\in(-3,0)
. For any smooth function
f
we have

\sup_{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|(v_{*})dv_{*}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{1}}^{1+\frac{\gamma}{3}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}^{-\frac{\gamma}{3}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{4}}.

Lemma 2.12
.

If
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, then for any smooth function
g
and
f
, we have

\mathcal{R}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}|f|^{2}dv_{*}dv\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}.

Proof.

The case
\gamma\geq 0
is obvious so we focus on the case
\gamma<0
. Since
\langle v\rangle^{|\gamma|}\lesssim\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|}\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|}
, together with Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{\langle v-v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|}}{|v-v_{*}|^{|\gamma|}}g_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|}|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}dv_{*}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(1+|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma})(g_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{|\gamma|})(f\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2})^{2}dv_{*}dv

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|}}+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2},

so the lemma is thus proved.
∎

Lemma 2.13
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,\gamma>-3,s\in(0,1)
. For any smooth function
g,f
and
h
, let

\mathcal{R}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}fh^{\prime}dv_{*}dvd\sigma,

then we have

(10)

\mathcal{R}\lesssim\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\},

and

(11)

\mathcal{R}\lesssim\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{k-\gamma/2-3/2}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+2}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}},\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}\}.

Proof.

The proof is just the combination of Lemma
2.2
, Lemma
2.10
and Lemma L212.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

(
[
9
]
, Lemma 2.2) Suppose
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
f\in H_{v}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
smooth, then we have

\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\alpha}(\langle v\rangle^{-2}f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq C\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})}.

Lemma 2.15
.

(
[
12
]
, Lemma 2.1) Suppose
H\in W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. Then for any
s\in(0,1)
, it holds that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(H^{\prime}-H)b(\cos\theta)d\sigma\leq C\left(\sup_{|u|\leq|v_{*}|+|v|}|\nabla H(u)|+\sup_{|u|\leq|v_{*}|+|v|}|\nabla^{2}H(u)|\right)|v-v_{*}|^{2}.

Lemma 2.16
.

(
[
9
]
, Proposition 2.11) Let
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
, then for some
r=r(\eta,\eta^{\prime},d)>2
and
\alpha=\alpha(\eta,\eta^{\prime},d)\in(0,1)
, for any smooth function
f
we have

\|f\|_{L^{r}_{x,v}}\leq C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{\eta}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dv\right)^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
f
.

Lemma 2.17
.

(
[
9
]
, Proposition 2.12) Let
\eta,\eta^{\prime}\in(0,1),m\geq 1
, then for some
r=\tilde{r}(\eta,\eta^{\prime},m,d)>2
and
\alpha=\tilde{\alpha}(\eta,\eta^{\prime},m,d)\in(0,1)
, for any smooth function
f
we have

\|f\|_{L^{r}_{x,v}}\leq C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{\eta}{2}}f\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{\eta^{\prime}}{2}}f^{2}\|_{L^{m}_{x}}^{2}dv\right)^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
f
and
\alpha
is continuous with
m
.

Lemma 2.18
.

(
[
9
]
, Proposition 2.13) Let
p\in(1,2)
,
0\leq\beta^{\prime}<\beta\in(0,1)
,
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p}
, then for any smooth function
f
we have

\|(-\Delta)^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2}}f^{2}\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C(\|f\|_{H^{\beta}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\|f^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}^{\frac{1}{2}}+\|f^{2}\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}),

where the constant C is independent of
f
.

We then introduce the time averaging lemma.

Lemma 2.19
.

(
[
9
]
, Proposition 2.14) Fix
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2},p\in(1,\infty),\beta>0
and assume that
f\in C([T_{1},T_{2}],L^{p}_{x,v})
with
\Delta_{v}^{\beta/2}\in L^{p}_{t,x,v}
satisfies

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=F,\quad t\in(0,+\infty),

Then for any
r\in[0,\frac{1}{p}],m\in{\mathbb{N}},\beta_{-}\in[0,\beta)
if we define

s^{b}=\frac{(1-rp)\beta_{-}}{p(1+m+\beta)},\quad\tilde{f}=f1_{(T_{1},T_{2})}(t),\quad\tilde{F}=F1_{(T_{1},T_{2})}(t),

then it follows that

\displaystyle\|(-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{s_{b}}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\langle v\rangle^{1+m}(1-\Delta_{x})^{-\frac{r}{2}}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{m}{2}}f(T_{1})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{1+m}(1-\Delta_{x})^{-\frac{r}{2}}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{m}{2}}f(T_{2})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\|\langle v\rangle^{1+m}(1-\Delta_{x}-\partial_{t}^{2})^{-\frac{r}{2}}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\frac{m}{2}}\tilde{F}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\frac{\beta}{2}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|\tilde{f}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
f
and
F
.

Lemma 2.20
.

(
[
9
]
, Lemma 3.5)
For any smooth function
f,g
, denote
G=\mu+g,F=\mu+f
, suppose
f
satisfies the linearized Boltzmann equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=2\tilde{Q}(G,F).

Then for any
j,l\geq 0,\tau>0,K>0,0\leq T_{1}<T_{2}\leq T
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}(\cdot,\cdot,v)\right|dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}(T_{1},\cdot,v)+2\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\left[\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}(\cdot,\cdot,v)\right]^{+}dxdt.

where
[\cdot]^{+}
denotes the positive part of the term and
f_{K,+}^{l}
is defined in (
5
).

Similarly we have

Lemma 2.21
.

(
[
9
]
Lemma 3.6)
For any smooth function
g,h
, denote
G=\mu+g
, suppose
h
satisfies the linearized Boltzmann equation

\partial_{t}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h=\tilde{Q}(G,-\mu+h),

then for any
j,l\geq 0,\tau>0,K>0,0\leq T_{1}<T_{2}\leq T
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}\tilde{Q}(G,-\mu+h)\langle v\rangle^{l}h_{K,+}^{l}(\cdot,\cdot,v)\right|dxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(h_{K,+}^{l})^{2}(T_{1},\cdot,v)+2\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\left[\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}\tilde{Q}(G,-\mu+h)\langle v\rangle^{l}h_{K,+}^{l}(\cdot,\cdot,v)\right]^{+}dxdt,

where
[\cdot]^{+}
denotes the positive part of the term.

3.
Estimates for the collision operator

In this section we focus on the estimates for the collisional operator
Q
. We first recall

Lemma 3.1
.

(
[
11
]
, Lemma 3.1)
Suppose
\gamma>-3,s\in(0,1)
, for any
l\geq 8
large,
h,g
smooth, we have

\displaystyle|(Q(h,\mu),g\langle v\rangle^{2l})|\leq

\displaystyle\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{l-\frac{3+\gamma}{2}}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}+C_{l}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}+C_{l}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}},

for some constant
C_{l}>0
.

Then we introduce several upper bounds for the weighted commutator which is very important in the whole paper. Various upper bounds handle the moments required in various estimates.

Lemma 3.2
.

Suppose
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1)
,
\gamma+2s>-1,l\geq 8
, for any smooth function
g,f,h
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)g(v_{*})f(v)h^{\prime}(v)\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\}

(12)

\displaystyle+\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}\},

with
s^{\prime}=\min\{\frac{1}{2},1-s\}>0
. We also have another estimate of
\Lambda

(13)

|\Lambda|\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{4}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+2}}.

Similarly we have

\displaystyle\Gamma:=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)g(v_{*})f(v)h^{\prime}(v)\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

(14)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},

we also have two other estimates of
\Gamma

(15)

|\Gamma|\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{4}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+2}},

and

(16)

|\Gamma|\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}(\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}+\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}})\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l+2}}.

Proof.

By (
10
)

\displaystyle|\Gamma-\Lambda|:=|\Lambda_{4}|=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)g(v_{*})f(v)h^{\prime}(v)\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}(1-\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}g_{*}|f|\langle v\rangle^{l}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\}.

By regular change of variable and Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{4}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}g_{*}|f|\langle v\rangle^{l}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}g_{*}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}g_{*}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{8}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}},

Gathering the two estimates we have

|\Lambda_{4}|\lesssim\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{8}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}\},

We focus on the
\Lambda
term. By Lemma
2.8
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(l\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|v-v_{*}|(v_{*}\cdot\omega)\cos^{l-1}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2})g_{*}fh^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}fh^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\sum_{i=2}^{3}L_{i}dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Lambda_{i},

with

|L_{1}|\leq C_{l}\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}\langle v\rangle^{2},\quad|L_{2}|\leq C_{l}\langle v\rangle^{l-2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}.

We first estimate the
\Lambda_{2}
term, for the
\Lambda_{2}
term, we have several different estimates. Since
l\geq 5
, by (
11
) we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}|f|\langle v\rangle^{2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},

Using (
10
) we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}|f|\langle v\rangle^{2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l+1}|f|\langle v\rangle^{3}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+|\gamma|+5}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}.

By singular change of variable we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}|f|\langle v\rangle^{2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\sin^{l-2-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}|f|\langle v\rangle^{2}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f|\langle v\rangle^{2}|h_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*},

by Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality we have

|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{p}_{2}}\|h\|_{L^{p}_{l}}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{4}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+2}},

where
p=1
if
\gamma=0
and
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{2}(2+\frac{\gamma}{3})\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
if
-3<\gamma<0
. By Lemma
2.11
we have

|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{6}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}},

Gathering the terms we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+|\gamma|+5}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{4}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{6}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}\}.

For the
\Lambda_{3}
term, by Lemma
2.13
we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{3}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}g_{*}|f|\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{5}g_{*}|f|\langle v\rangle^{l-1}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\}.

By regular change of variable and Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{3}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}|f|\langle v\rangle^{l}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{8}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}.

Gathering the two estimates we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{3}|\lesssim\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{8}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}\},

For the
\Lambda_{1}
term, by (
9
) we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(l\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|v-v_{*}|(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2})g_{*}fh^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(l\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|v-v_{*}|(v_{*}\cdot\frac{v^{\prime}-v_{*}}{|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|})\cos^{l-1}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2})g_{*}fh^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,1}+\Lambda_{1,2}.

For the
\Lambda_{1,2}
term, using (
10
) we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle|g_{*}||f|\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}|g_{*}||f|\langle v\rangle^{l-1}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\}.

By regular change of variable and Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle|g_{*}||f|\langle v\rangle^{l-2}|h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{3}|g_{*}||h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}.

Gathering the two estimates we have

|\Lambda_{1,2}|\lesssim\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l}}\}.

For the
\Lambda_{1,1}
term, by Lemma
2.9
we have

(17)

\Lambda_{1,1}=l\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}g_{*}(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-2})h^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

Using

f\langle v\rangle^{l-2}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-2}=\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}(f\langle v\rangle^{l-s}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s})+f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s}\left(\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}-\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}}\right),

we split
\Lambda_{1,1}
into two parts

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,1}=

\displaystyle l\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}g_{*}h^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}(f\langle v\rangle^{l-s}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+l\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}(v_{*}\cdot\tilde{\omega})\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}g_{*}f^{\prime}h^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-s}\left(\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}-\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}}\right)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,1,1}+\Lambda_{1,1,2}.

For the
\Lambda_{1,1,2}
term, by

\left|\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}-\frac{1}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}}\right|=\frac{|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}-\langle v\rangle^{2-s}|}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}\lesssim\frac{|v^{\prime}-v|(\langle v\rangle^{1-s}+\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{1-s})}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}\lesssim\frac{|v^{\prime}-v|\langle v\rangle^{1-s}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{1-s}}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}\langle v\rangle^{2-s}}\lesssim\frac{|v-v_{*}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle}{\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2-s}\langle v\rangle},

together with regular change of variable and (
10
) we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,1,2}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}|g_{*}|\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle}|f^{\prime}||h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{3}|g_{*}||f^{\prime}||h^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-3}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}\cos^{l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{3}|g_{*}||f||h|\langle v\rangle^{2l-3}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\cos^{l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{5}|g_{*}||f||h|\langle v\rangle^{2l-1}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}},\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2-1/2}}\},

For the
\Lambda_{1,1,1}
term, by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,1,1}|\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}|g_{*}|(f\langle v\rangle^{l-s}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s})^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma+2}}{\langle v\rangle^{4-2s}}\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right)^{1/2}.

By

\frac{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{\langle v\rangle^{4-2s}}\lesssim\frac{\langle v\rangle^{2}+\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}}{\langle v\rangle^{4-2s}}\lesssim\frac{\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}}{\langle v\rangle^{2-2s}},

together with regular change of variable and Lemma
2.12
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\frac{|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma+2}}{\langle v\rangle^{4}}\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|g_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}|g_{*}|\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{2-2s}}|h^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}|g_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2+2s}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)\cos^{2l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v^{\prime}-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{4}|g_{*}||h^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2+2s}dv^{\prime}dv_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+8}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-1+s}}^{2}\}.

Since
(a-b)^{2}=-2a(b-a)+(b^{2}-a^{2})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}(f\langle v\rangle^{l-s}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s})^{2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}f\langle v\rangle^{l-s}(f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-s}-f\langle v\rangle^{l-s})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}(|f^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2s}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l-2s})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(Q(|g|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2},f\langle\cdot\rangle^{l-s}),f\langle\cdot\rangle^{l-s})+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}(|f^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2s}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l-2s})dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

By cancellation lemma and Lemma
2.12
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}(|f^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l-2s}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l-2s})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|g_{*}|\langle v_{*}\rangle^{2}|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l-2s}dvdv_{*}\lesssim\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2-s}}^{2},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+8}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s}}^{2}\},

and by Lemma
2.3
we have

|(Q(|g|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2},f\langle\cdot\rangle^{l-s}),f\langle\cdot\rangle^{l-s})|\lesssim(\|g\|_{L^{1}_{|\gamma|+5}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+5}})\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

For the term
\Lambda_{1,1}
we have another estimate, recall (
17
), by mean value theorem

|f\langle v\rangle^{l-2}-f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l-2}|\leq\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}|v-v^{\prime}|\leq\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}|v-v_{*}|\sin\frac{\theta}{2},

together with regular change of variable and Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,1}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}g_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle h^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}\cos^{l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}g_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle h\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{3}h\langle v\rangle^{l+2}dvdv_{*}d\sigma\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{7}}\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l+2}},

Gathering the estimates for
\Lambda_{1,1,1}
and
\Lambda_{1,1,2}
terms we have

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1,1}|\lesssim

\displaystyle\min\{\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{7}}\|\nabla(f\langle v\rangle^{l-2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|h\|_{L^{1}_{l+2}}\},

with
s^{\prime}=\min\{\frac{1}{2},1-s\}>0
. The lemma is proved by gathering all the terms together.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Suppose
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. For any smooth function
f,g
, for any small constant
\epsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle\Lambda=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}b(\cos\theta)\mu(v_{*})g(v)f^{\prime}(v)\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\|\mu\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+|\gamma|+5}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}\leq C_{l}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}\leq\epsilon\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2},

for some constant
C_{l,\epsilon}>0
, where
s^{\prime}=\min\{\frac{1}{2},1-s\}
.

Theorem 3.4
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. For any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g
satisfies

G\geq 0,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}}\geq A,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L}\leq B,

for some constant
A,B>0
. For any
l\geq 10
we have

\displaystyle(Q(G,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})\leq

\displaystyle-\gamma_{2}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\|b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\|_{L^{1}_{\theta}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2},

for some constant
\gamma_{2},C_{l}>0
, where
s^{\prime}=\min\{\frac{1}{2},1-s\}>0
.

Proof.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

|f||f^{\prime}|\langle v\rangle^{l}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l}\leq\frac{1}{2}(|f^{\prime}|^{2}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l}\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l}),

so by cancellation lemma we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f)f\langle v\rangle^{2l}dv=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}(ff^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{2l}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}(|f||f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}(|f||f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}|f||f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}|f|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{2l}(\cos^{2l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}-1)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}|f||f^{\prime}|\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=T_{1}+T_{2}.

First we talk on
T_{1}
term, by
G=\mu+g\geq 0
, by Lemma
2.12
we have

T_{1}\leq-\gamma_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2},

for some constant
\gamma_{0},C_{l}>0
, where
\gamma_{0}
is defined by

(18)

\gamma_{0}:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}b(\cos\theta)\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}d\sigma\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}b(\cos\theta)(1-\cos^{2l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2})d\sigma.

For the
T_{2}
term, by (
3.2
) and Corollary
3.3
we have

T_{2}\leq\epsilon\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

which implies

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}\leq

\displaystyle-\gamma_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+\epsilon\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

We introduce another decomposition

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f)f\langle v\rangle^{2l}dv=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f\langle v\rangle^{l})f\langle v\rangle^{l}dv+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left(\langle v\rangle^{l}Q(G,f)-Q(G,f\langle v\rangle^{l})\right)f\langle v\rangle^{l}dv:=T_{3}+T_{4}.

For the
T_{3}
term, by Lemma
2.4
we have

T_{3}\leq-\gamma_{1}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

for some constant
\gamma_{1}>0
. For
T_{4}
term, we split it into three parts

\displaystyle T_{4}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}G_{*}ff^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}\mu_{*}ff^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}\mu_{*}f\langle v\rangle^{l}f^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}-1)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{r}g_{*}ff^{\prime}\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=T_{4,1}+T_{4,2}+T_{4,3}.

For the
T_{4,1},T_{4,2}
term, by Corollary
3.3
and Lemma
2.10
we have

|T_{4,1}|\leq\epsilon\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2},\quad|T_{4,2}|\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

For
T_{4,3}
term by (
3.2
) we have

|T_{4,3}|\leq C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

which implies

T_{4}\leq\epsilon\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

Taking
\epsilon=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle T_{3}+T_{4}\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}.

Gathering the two ways of expansion linearly, take
\delta_{2}>0
small we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f)f\langle v\rangle^{2l}dv\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\delta_{2}}(T_{1}+T_{2})+\frac{\delta_{2}}{1+\delta_{2}}(T_{3}+T_{4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\delta_{2}}(\epsilon-\delta_{2}\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2})\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{1+\delta_{2}}(-\gamma_{0}+C_{l}\delta_{2})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta_{2}\frac{\gamma_{1}}{8}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2,*}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\min\{\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|g\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}},\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}\}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2},

by taking
\delta_{2}=\min\{\frac{\gamma_{0}}{4C_{l}},\frac{1}{2}\}
,
\epsilon=\frac{\gamma_{1}\delta_{2}}{4}
. The proof is thus finished.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

For any
0\leq\theta\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
,
l>10
, we have

\frac{1}{4}\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}-\sin^{l-2}\frac{\theta}{2}\geq\sin^{2}\frac{\theta}{2}\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2^{\frac{l-2}{2}}}\right)>0,

Combine Lemma
3.1
and Theorem
3.4
, by Remark
3.5
we have

Theorem 3.6
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. For any smooth function
f
, suppose
F=\mu+f
satisfies

F\geq 0,\quad\|F\|_{L^{1}}\geq A,\quad\|F\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|F\|_{L\log L}\leq B,

for some constant
A,B>0
. For any
l>10
, there exist constants
c_{0},C_{l}>0
such that

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+f,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})

\displaystyle\leq-4c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-2c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

Moreover, if
\chi
is the cutoff function defined in (
7
), we still have

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+f\chi,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})

\displaystyle\leq-4c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-2c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}.

Combine Lemma
3.1
and Theorem
3.4
, we also have

Theorem 3.7
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. For any smooth function
g
, suppose
G=\mu+g
satisfies

G\geq 0,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}}\geq A,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L}\leq B,

for some constant
A,B>0
. For any
l>10
, there exist constants
c_{0},C_{l}>0
such that

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+g,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})\leq

\displaystyle-4c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}.

For the regularizing linear operator defined in (
6
) we have

Lemma 3.8
.

(
[
9
]
, Proposition 3.1 ) For any
\alpha\geq 0,l\geq 8
, for any function
f
smooth, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)f\langle v\rangle^{2l}dvdx\leq-\frac{1}{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\alpha}}^{2}-\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{l+\alpha}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}.

Lemma 3.9
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g
satisfies

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

for some constant
A,B>0
. If
f
is the solution to

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\tilde{Q}(\mu+g,\mu+f)=Q(\mu+g,\mu+f)+\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f),\quad\epsilon\in[0,1],

Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
\alpha\geq 0
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8
, suppose

\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}<\delta_{0},\quad\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}<+\infty,

for some
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then for any
t\geq 0
have

(19)

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau+\leq Ce^{C(g)t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2}t+\epsilon^{2}t),

for some constant
c_{0}>0
, where

C(g)=1+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2}.

If in addition we assume
l\geq 3+2\alpha
, then for any
0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}
, we have

(20)

\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau\leq Ce^{C(g)t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2}t+\epsilon^{2}t),

where the constant
C
is independent of
\epsilon
.

Proof.

Since
\gamma\leq 0
, by Theorem
3.7
and Lemma
3.8
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}=

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+g,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\epsilon(L_{\alpha}(\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}+\epsilon C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\epsilon C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(2c_{0}-C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}+\epsilon)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}

(21)

\displaystyle+C_{l}(\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}+\epsilon)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l+\gamma/2}}

(22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-c_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l}}^{2}+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2},

by Grönwall’s inequality we deduce (
19
). For (
20
), by Lemma
2.3
we have

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}({Q}(G,F))\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-s}({Q}(G,f)+Q(f,\mu))\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{2}_{7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}}+\|g\|_{L^{2}_{7}}+\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}},

and

\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\leq\epsilon\|f\|_{L^{2}_{3+2\alpha}}+C\epsilon.

By Lemma
2.19
with

\beta=s,\quad m=2,\quad r=0,\quad p=2,\quad s^{b}=\frac{s_{-}}{2(s+3)}:=s^{\prime},\quad T_{1}=0,\quad T_{2}=T,

where
s_{-}
is any constant satisfies
0<s_{-}<s
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-1}f(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-1}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)(\tau))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{3}f(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle^{3}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau

(23)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)(\tau))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau,

the proof is thus finished if we assume
l\geq 3+2\alpha
.
∎

Corollary 3.10
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f
, suppose
f
is a solution to the modified Boltzmann equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+f\chi,\mu+f),

where
\chi
is the cutoff function defined in (
7
). Suppose
F=\mu+f\chi
satisfies

F\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|F\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|F\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

For some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l
, suppose

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}<\delta_{0},

for some
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then for any
t\geq 0
have

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(\tau)\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}dxd\tau\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l}t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}t),

for some constant
c_{0},C_{l}>0
. If in addition
l\geq 3+2\alpha
, then for any
0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}
, we have

\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}dt\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l}t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}t).

Proof.

Since
\gamma\leq 0
, by Theorem
3.7
and Lemma
3.8
we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}=

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+f\chi,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\epsilon(L_{\alpha}(\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+\epsilon C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+\epsilon C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}},

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(2c_{0}-C_{l}\sup_{x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7+|\gamma|}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+(C_{l}+\epsilon)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\epsilon\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-c_{0}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\epsilon^{2},

the remaining proof follows a similar line as for Lemma
3.9
and thus omitted.
∎

4.
Estimate for the level set function

In this section, we focus on the linearized equation

(24)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\tilde{Q}(\mu+g,\mu+f)=Q(\mu+g,\mu+f)+\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f),\quad\epsilon\in[0,1],\quad f(0,x,v)=f_{0}(x,v),\quad\mu+f\geq 0.

In this section we come to compute the level set estimate for the Boltzmann equation. It is easily seen that if
f
is a solution to (
24
), the level set function
f_{k,+}^{l}
defined in (
5
) satisfies

(25)

\partial_{t}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}=2\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}.

We first prove a bound for the level set function

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose
G=\mu+g,F=\mu+f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Suppose in addition G satisfies that

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty.

For some constant
A,B>0
.

(1) For any constant
l>10,K\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx\leq

\displaystyle-(2c_{1}-C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{2}}^{2}

(26)

\displaystyle+C_{l}(1+K)(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}},

for some constant
c_{1},C_{l}>0
.

(2) For the regularizing term, for any
l\geq 10,\alpha,K\geq 0
we have

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx\leq-\frac{1}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},

for some constant
C_{l,\alpha}>0
.

Proof.

The regularizing term is proved in
[
9
]
Proposition 3.3. We focus on the collision term later. First make the decomposition

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\mu)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv:=T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}.

Recall the fact that

f\langle v\rangle^{l}-K\leq f_{K,+}^{l}(v),\quad(f\langle v\rangle^{l}-K)f_{K,+}^{l}(v)=|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2},\quad f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\geq 0,

since
G\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\displaystyle f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})

\displaystyle=
