\displaystyle\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{l})+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}),

together with cancellation lemma we have

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\left(|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}\right)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}(\cos^{2l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}-1)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}(\cos^{2l-3-\gamma}\frac{\theta}{2}-1)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mu_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}g_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}+T_{1,3}+T_{1,4}.

By cancellation lemma and Lemma
2.11
we have

T_{1,1}\leq-\gamma_{0}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}},\quad T_{1,2}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+4}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}},

where
\gamma_{0}
is defined in (
18
). For the term
T_{1,3}
, by Lemma
2.3
apply for
f=f_{K,+}^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}
we have

T_{1,3}\lesssim\epsilon\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}.

For the term
T_{1,4}
, by (
13
) apply for
f=h=f_{K,+}^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}
we have

T_{1,4}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{3-l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}.

We also have another way on estimate
T_{1}
term, by

(27)

f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})=f_{K,+}^{l}(v)(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}(v))+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}),

we have

\displaystyle T_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=T_{1,5}+T_{1,6}.

By Lemma
2.4
we have

T_{1,5}=(Q(G,f_{K,+}^{l}),f_{K,+}^{l})\leq-\gamma_{1}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2},

for some constant
\gamma_{1}>0
. For the term
T_{1,6}
, by (
15
) apply for
f=h=f_{K,+}^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}
we have

T_{1,6}\lesssim\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{3-l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}.

Gathering the two estimates, for some
\delta_{2}\in(0,1)
small we compute

\displaystyle T_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+\delta_{2}}(T_{1,1}+T_{1,2}+T_{1,3}+T_{1,4})+\frac{\delta_{2}}{1+\delta_{2}}(T_{1,5}+T_{1,6})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{-\gamma_{0}+C_{l}\delta_{2}}{1+\delta_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+\frac{-\gamma_{1}\delta_{2}+\epsilon}{1+\delta_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{3-l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(2c_{1}-C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2},

for some
c_{1}>0
by taking
\delta_{2}=\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2C_{l}},\epsilon=\frac{\gamma_{1}\delta_{2}}{2}
. For the
T_{2}
term, using (
27
) we first split it into two terms

\displaystyle T_{2}=

\displaystyle K\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+K\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=T_{2,1}+T_{2,2}.

For the term
T_{2,1}
, by cancellation lemma and Lemma
2.11
we have

T_{2,1}\lesssim K\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)dvdv_{*}\lesssim K\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{4}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim K(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}.

For the term
T_{2,2}
, by (
16
) we have

T_{2,2}\lesssim K\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\lesssim K(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}.

For the
T_{3}
term, similarly we have

\displaystyle T_{3}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\mu(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\mu\langle v\rangle^{l}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\mu f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=T_{3,1}+T_{3,2}.

For the term
T_{3,1}
by cancellation lemma we have

T_{3,1}\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|dvdv_{*}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{4}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}.

For the term
T_{3,2}
, by (
16
) we have

T_{3,2}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\lesssim(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}},

gathering the terms together we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv\leq-(c_{1}-C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)(l+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}},

by integrating in
x
, (
4.1
) is proved .
∎

To show
|f|\langle v\rangle^{k}\leq K
later, we will need not only the level set function
f_{K,+}^{l}
, but also the one for
-f\langle v\rangle^{l}\leq K
. It is not surprising that the estimate for
(-f)_{K,+}^{l}
follows a similar line as that for
f_{K,+}^{l}
. The equation for
h=-f
is

\partial_{t}h+v\cdot\nabla_{x}h=-Q(G,\mu-h)-\epsilon L_{\alpha}(u-h),

for the function
h
we have the following estimate

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose
G=\mu+g
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Suppose in addition G satisfies that

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty.

For some constant
A,B>0
.

(1) For any constant
l>10,K\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\mu-h)h_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx\leq

\displaystyle-(c_{1}-C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{2}}^{2}

(28)

\displaystyle+C_{l}(1+K)(l+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}},

for some constant
c_{1},C_{l}>0
.

(2) For the regularizing term, for any
l\geq 10,\alpha,K\geq 0
we have

-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(\mu-h)h_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx\leq-\frac{1}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}(1+K)\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}},

for some constant
C_{l,\alpha}>0
.

Proof.

The regularizing term is proved in
[
9
]
Proposition 3.4. We focus on the term later. First make the decomposition

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\mu-h)h_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,h-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})h_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})h_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\mu)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dv:=K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3},

The
K_{1}

K_{2}
term is the same as
T_{1},T_{2}
term in Lemma
4.1
, we have

K_{1}+K_{2}\leq-(c_{1}-C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}^{2}+C_{l}K(l+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|h_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}},

For the
K_{3}
term, similarly as
T_{3}
term in Lemma
4.1
we have

\displaystyle K_{3}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(-\mu)(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(-\mu)\langle v\rangle^{l}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S^{2}}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(-\mu)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l})dvdv_{*}d\sigma:=K_{3,1}+K_{3,2}.

For the term
K_{3,1}
, by (
16
) we have

K_{3,1}\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|dvdv_{*}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{4}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}\lesssim(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}}.

For the term
K_{3,2}
by Lemma
2.3
we have

K_{3,2}\lesssim\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\lesssim(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{2}},

the proof is ended by gathering all the terms and integrate in
x
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. For any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g
satisfies

G\geq 0,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}}\geq A,\quad\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L}\leq B,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
T>0
and

[T_{1},T_{2}]\in[0,T],\quad\epsilon\in[0,1],\quad j\geq 0,\quad\tau>2,\quad K\geq 0,\quad l\geq 10,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\right|dvdxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{j/2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C(1+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C(1+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}L^{2}_{j+2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C(1+K)(1+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}L^{1}_{j+2}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
\epsilon
. Identical estimate holds for
\tilde{Q}(G,-\mu+h)
with
f_{K,+}^{l}
replaced by
h_{K,+}^{l}

Proof.

First note that for any
\tau\geq 0
, by the theory of Bessel kernel

(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}f=G_{\tau}*f,\quad G_{\tau}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}),

which implies

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}(T_{1},x,v)dxdv\leq C\|\langle v\rangle^{j/2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

By Lemma
2.20
we have that for any
j\geq 0,l\geq 0,\tau\geq 0

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\right|dvdxdt-C\|\langle v\rangle^{l/2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1},\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left[\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\right]^{+}dvdxdt

\displaystyle=2\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})1_{A_{K}}dvdxdt

\displaystyle=2\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\langle v\rangle^{j}1_{A_{K}})dvdxdt,

where
A_{K}
is the set given by

A_{K}=\{(t,x,v)\in(T_{1},T_{2})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}|(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\geq 0\}.

Denote

(29)

W_{K}(v):=(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\langle v\rangle^{j}1_{A_{K}})\geq 0,

since
\tau>2
, by (3.39) in
[
9
]
we have

|W_{K}(v)|+|\nabla_{i}W_{K}(v)|+|\nabla^{2}_{i,j}W_{k}(v)|\leq C\langle v\rangle^{j},\quad i,j=1,2,3,

with
C>0
is independent of
K
. Then we only need to estimate the term

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}dvdxdt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}dvdxdt+\epsilon\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}dvdxdt,

For the second term, by
[
9
]
Proposition 3.7 we have

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}dvdx\leq C_{l,\alpha}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{j/2}}^{2}+C_{l,\alpha}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{j}}.

We focus on the collision term later. First make the decomposition

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}dv=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}dv+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}dv

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,\mu)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}dv:=T_{1}^{\prime}+T_{2}^{\prime}+T_{3}^{\prime}.

For the
T_{1}^{\prime}
term performing a similar argument we still have

\displaystyle T_{1}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}(f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K}(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle f_{K,+}^{l}(v)\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}(v)W_{k}(v)\langle v\rangle^{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{K})+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}W_{K}(v^{\prime})\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}W_{K}(v)\right)+\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{K}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}(W_{K}(v^{\prime})-W_{K}(v)),

we split
T_{1}^{\prime}
term into 3 terms
T_{1}^{\prime}\leq T_{1,1}^{\prime}+T_{1,2}^{\prime}+T_{1,3}^{\prime}
respectively. For the term
T_{1,1}^{\prime}
by cancellation lemma and Lemma
2.12
we have

\displaystyle T_{1,1}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\left(|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}W_{k}(v^{\prime})\cos^{2l}\frac{\theta}{2}-|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}W_{k}(v)\right)dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\gamma_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(\mu+g)(f_{K,+}^{l}(v))^{2}W_{k}(v)dvdv_{*}\leq-\gamma_{0}(1-C_{l}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{4+|\gamma|}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\sqrt{W_{K}}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}.

where
\gamma_{0}
is defined in (
18
). For the
T_{1,2}^{\prime}
term by (
16
) we have

\displaystyle T_{1,2}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}}f_{K,+}^{l}(v)f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})W_{k}(v^{\prime})(\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-\langle v\rangle^{l}\cos^{l}\frac{\theta}{2})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma/2}}+\|G\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{3-l}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{j+2}}^{2}.

For the
T_{1,3}^{\prime}
term by Lemma
2.15
and Lemma
2.11
we have

\displaystyle T_{1,3}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v)|^{2}(W_{K}^{\prime}-W_{K})dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}G_{*}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}\left(\sup_{|u|\leq|v_{*}|+|v|}|\nabla W_{K}(u)|+\sup_{|u|\leq|v_{*}|+|v|}|\nabla^{2}W_{K}(u)|\right)dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}G_{*}\langle v_{*}\rangle^{j+2}|f_{K,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{j+2}dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{j+6}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{j/2+1}}^{2}.

The term
T_{2}^{\prime},T_{3}^{\prime}
is the same as the term
T_{2},T_{3}
in Lemma
4.1
, thus we have

|T_{2}^{\prime}|+|T_{3}^{\prime}|\lesssim C(1+K)(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}W_{K}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\lesssim C(1+K)(1+\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{j+2}},

so the proof is ended by gathering all the terms together and integrate in
t
and
x
.
∎

For any
l\geq 0,p>1,0<s^{\prime\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)},K\geq 0,0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}
fixed, we define the energy functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(K,T_{1},T_{2}):=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dxd\tau

(30)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{1/p}.

where
C_{0}
is a constant which will be determined later.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let the parameters
T_{1},T_{2},s,s^{\prime\prime},l,n
be given such that

0\leq T_{1}<T_{2}<T,\quad 0<s^{\prime\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}\in(0,1),\quad l\geq 0,\quad n\geq 0.

There exist a constant
p^{c}>1
depends on
s,s^{\prime\prime}
such that for any
p\in(1,p^{c})
fixed, there exist a
l_{0}>0
large such that if we suppose

\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+l_{0}}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{1},

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Then there exist a constant
q_{*}
which is independent of
p
and satisfies
1<q_{*}<\frac{r(1)}{2}
such that the following holds: For any
1<q<q_{*}
we can find a pair of parameter
(r_{*},\xi_{*})
with the properties

r_{*}>q_{*}>q>1,\quad\xi_{*}>2q_{*}>2q>2,

such that for any
0\leq M<K
and
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{n}{q}}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{q}((T_{1},T_{2})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq C\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\frac{r_{*}}{q}}}{(K-M)^{\frac{\xi_{*}-2q}{q}}},

where C only depends on
(C_{1},s,s^{\prime\prime},q,p)
and
q_{*}
only depends on
(s,s^{\prime\prime})
,
r_{*},\xi_{*}
only depends on
(s,s^{\prime\prime},p)
and
l_{0}
only depends on
(s,s^{\prime\prime},p,n)
. In particular, all these parameters are independent of
K,M,T_{1},T_{2},l
and
f
.

Proof.

For any
p>1
, define

r(1):=\tilde{r}(s,s^{\prime\prime},1,3),\quad r(p):=\tilde{r}(s,s^{\prime\prime},p,3),

where
\tilde{r}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in Lemma
2.17
. By the continuity of
r(\cdot)
,

\frac{r(z)}{2z}\frac{r(1)-2}{r(z)-2}\to\frac{r(1)}{2}>1,\quad\hbox{as}\quad z\to 1,

there exist a
p^{c}\in(1,2)
fixed and close enough to
1
such that

(31)

\min_{[1,p^{c}]}\frac{r(p)}{2p}\frac{r(1)-2}{r(p)-2}>1.

Choose

q_{*}:=1+\frac{r(1)-2}{2r(p^{c})},\quad 1<q_{*}<r(1)/2,

it is clear that
q_{*}
only depends on
p^{c},s,s^{\prime\prime}
(independent of
p
). Such choice guarantees that

(32)

\frac{r(p^{c})}{2}\frac{2q_{*}-2}{r(1)-2}=\frac{1}{2}<1.

For any
\xi,q
satisfying
2<2q<\xi<r(1)<r(p)
, define
\beta=\beta(\xi,p)\in(0,1)
by

\frac{1}{\xi}=\frac{1-\beta}{2}+\frac{\beta}{r(p)},\quad\beta\in(0,1),\quad\beta\xi=\frac{r(p)(\xi-2)}{r(p)-2}.

By (
32
) we have

\frac{\beta\xi}{2p}<\frac{\beta\xi}{2}=\frac{r(p)}{2}\frac{\xi-2}{r(p)-2}\leq\frac{r(p^{c})}{2}\frac{\xi-2}{r(1)-2}<1,\quad\hbox{as}\quad\xi\to 2q_{*},\quad\xi>2q_{*},\quad\forall p\in(1,p^{c}),

and by (
31
) we have

\frac{\beta\xi}{2}>\frac{\beta\xi}{2p}=\frac{r(p)}{2p}\frac{\xi-2}{r(p)-2}\to\frac{r(p)}{2p}\frac{r(1)-2}{r(p)-2}>1,\quad\hbox{as}\quad\xi\to r(1),\quad\xi<r(1),\quad\forall p\in(1,p^{c}).

So for any
\zeta\in(0,1)
, for any
p\in(1,p^{c})
we can find a pair
\beta_{*},\xi_{*}
such that

\zeta\frac{\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}{2}+(1-\zeta)\frac{\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}{2p}=1,\quad\frac{1}{\xi_{*}}=\frac{1-\beta_{*}}{2}+\frac{\beta_{*}}{r(p)}.

We will take
\zeta=\tilde{\alpha}(s,s^{\prime\prime},p,3)
later, where
\tilde{\alpha}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in Lemma
2.17
. Wwith the parameters above we can start prove the theorem. For any
q\in(1,q_{*})
, since
K>M>0
implies
f_{M,+}^{l}\geq f_{K,+}^{l}+(K-M)
, by Hölder inequality we have

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{n}{q}}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{q}_{t,x,v}}^{q}=

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{n}{2q}}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2q}_{x,v}}^{2q}d\tau\leq\frac{1}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2q}}\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{n/\xi_{*}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{\xi_{*}}_{x,v}}^{\xi_{*}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2q}}\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma/2}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{r(p)}_{x,v}}^{\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}d\tau,

where
a_{0}=\frac{1}{1-\beta_{*}}(\frac{n}{\xi_{*}}+(4-\gamma/2)\beta_{*})
. By Lemma
2.17
with parameter
(r(p),s,s^{\prime\prime},p)
and Hölder’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma/2}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{r(p)}_{x,v}}^{\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma/2}f_{M,+}^{l})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\zeta\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-8+\gamma}(1-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{2}}(f_{M,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{v}L^{p}_{x}}^{\frac{1-\zeta}{2}\xi_{*}\beta_{*}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{M,+}^{l})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\zeta\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(1-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{2}}(f_{M,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{\frac{1-\zeta}{2}\xi_{*}\beta_{*}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\right)\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{M,+}^{l})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{\zeta\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(1-\Delta_{x})^{\frac{s^{\prime\prime}}{2}}(f_{M,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1-\zeta}{2p}\xi_{*}\beta_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\right)\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1}.T_{2})^{\frac{\zeta\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1}.T_{2})^{\frac{1-\zeta}{2}\xi_{*}\beta_{*}}.

By Hölder inequality we easily have

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\leq\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta^{\prime})(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\sup_{t}\|f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\beta^{\prime}(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(1-\beta^{\prime})(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta^{\prime}(1-\beta_{*})\frac{\xi_{*}}{2}},

where
l_{0}=\frac{a_{0}}{1-\beta^{\prime}}
. Gathering all the terms we deduce

\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{a_{0}}f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma/2}f\|_{L^{r(p)}_{x,v}}^{\beta_{*}\xi_{*}}d\tau\leq CC_{1}^{(1-\beta^{\prime})(1-\beta_{*})\xi_{*}}E_{P}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}},

with

r_{*}=(1-(1-\beta^{\prime})(1-\beta_{*}))\frac{\xi_{*}}{2},

since
\xi_{*}>2q_{*}
we can make
r_{*}>q_{*}
by taking
l_{0}\gg a_{0}
which could implies that
\beta^{\prime}
is arbitrarily near
1
. We can see that
r_{*},\xi_{*}
only depends on
s,s^{\prime\prime},p
and
l_{0}
only depends on
\beta_{*},\xi_{*},n,\beta^{\prime}
which only depends on
s,s^{\prime\prime},p,n
.
∎

With the lemma above, we are ready to estimate regarding the energy functional

Lemma 4.5
.

Suppose
G=\mu+g,F=\mu+f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T>0,\alpha\geq 0
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
24
). Suppose in addition G satisfies that

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}
, suppose

(33)

\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},\quad\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\leq C,

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then there exists
s">0
and
p^{a}>0
such that for any
p\in(1,p^{a})
there exist
l_{0}>0
which depends on
s^{\prime\prime},p
such that, if we assume

\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l_{0}+l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{1}<+\infty,

Then for any
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T,\epsilon\in(0,1),0\leq M<K
we have

\displaystyle\|f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{{s^{\prime\prime}}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{CK}{K-M}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta_{i}}}{(K-M)^{a_{i}}},

for some constant
\beta_{i}>1,a_{i}>0
are defined later. The constant
C
is independent of
\epsilon,K,M,f,T_{1},T_{2}
. Furthermore, the same estimate holds for
h=-f
with
f_{K,+}^{l}
replaced by
h_{K,+}^{l}
.

Proof.

If
f
is a solution to (
24
) we have
f_{K,+}^{l}
satisfies (
25
). Choose
\sigma=1/4,\tau_{1}>2
,
f_{K,+}^{l}
satisfies

\partial_{t}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}=2\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l-4+\gamma}f_{K,+}^{l}:=(1-\Delta_{x}-\partial_{t}^{2})^{\sigma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{\sigma/2+\tau_{1}/2}G_{K}^{l},

where we define
G_{K}^{l}
as

G_{K}^{l}:=2(1-\Delta_{x}-\partial_{t}^{2})^{-\sigma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\sigma/2-\tau_{1}/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l-4+\gamma}f_{K,+}^{l}).

Choose the parameters in Lemma
2.19
as

m=\tau_{1}+\sigma,\quad\beta\in(0,s),\quad s^{b}=s^{\prime\prime}=\frac{(1-2\sigma)\beta_{-}}{2(1+\sigma+\tau_{1}+\beta)}<\min\{\beta,\frac{(1-\sigma p)\beta_{-}}{p(1+\sigma+\tau_{1}+\beta)}\},\quad r=\sigma,\quad\tau_{1}>2,\quad\sigma+\tau_{1}<3,

where
1<p<2
is chosen to be close enough to 1 such that

1<p<p^{c},\quad\sigma p<1,\quad 1<p<\frac{p}{2-p}<q_{*},\quad\sigma p_{*}=\sigma\frac{p}{p-1}>6,

where
p^{c},q_{*}
is defined in Lemma
4.4
and the last condition guarantees that

H^{-\sigma,p}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\supseteq L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\quad\hbox{since}\quad H^{\sigma,p^{*}}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\subseteq L^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}).

With such choice, by Lemma
2.19
we have

\displaystyle\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1}))^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{4}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{k,+}^{l}(T_{2}))^{2}\|_{H^{-\sigma,p}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\|\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\beta/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{1+\sigma+\tau_{1}}G_{K}^{l}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{2+\gamma/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1}))\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{4}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{k,+}^{l}(T_{2}))^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle+\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\beta/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{4}G_{K}^{l}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}.

We will estimate the terms in the right hand side separately. For the third term by Lemma
4.4
we have

\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\frac{r_{*}}{p}}}{(K-M)^{\frac{\xi_{*}-2p}{p}}},\quad r_{*}>p,\quad\xi_{*}>2p.

For the fourth term, using Lemma
2.18
with
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p}
and Hölder’s inequality we have

\displaystyle\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\beta/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}^{p}=\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\beta/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{v}}^{p}dxd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{v}}^{p}\|(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{v}}^{\frac{p}{2}}+\|(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{v}}^{p}dxd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x,v}}^{p^{\prime}}d\tau\right)^{\frac{2-p}{2}}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau.

The first term is bounded by

\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{M,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau\leq\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2}).

Since
1<p<p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p}<q_{*}
, by Lemma
4.4
we have

\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x,v}}^{p^{\prime}}d\tau\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{{r_{*}}}}{(K-M)^{{\xi_{*}-2p^{\prime}}}},\quad\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{{r_{*}}}}{(K-M)^{{\xi_{*}-2p}}}.

Gathering all the terms we have

\|(-\Delta_{v})^{\beta/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta_{1}}}{(K-M)^{a_{1}}}+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta_{2}}}{(K-M)^{a_{2}}},

where the parameters satisfies

\beta_{1}=\frac{1}{2}(1+r_{*}/p^{\prime})>1,\quad a_{1}=(\xi-2p^{\prime})/(2p^{\prime})>0,\quad\beta_{2}=r_{*}/p>1,\quad a_{2}=(\xi_{*}-2p)/p>0.

Since
H^{-\sigma,p}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\supseteq L^{1}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, by Lemma
4.3
with
j=4
we have

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{4}G_{K}^{l}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{4}(1-\Delta_{x}-\partial_{t}^{2})^{-\sigma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\sigma/2-\tau_{1}/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l-4+\gamma}f_{K,+}^{l})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{6}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+(1+K)\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{6}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau,

by Lemma
4.4
we have

(34)

\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{6}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\leq\frac{2^{2p-2}}{(K-M)^{2p-2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{12}{2p}}f_{\frac{K+M}{2},+}^{l}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2p}d\tau\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2}},

similarly

(35)

\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{6}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau\leq\frac{2^{2p-1}}{(K-M)^{2p-1}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\frac{6}{2p}}f_{\frac{K+M}{2},+}^{l}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2p}d\tau\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}.

Since
\frac{K}{K-M}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle(1+K)\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}+\frac{K}{K-M}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2}}

(36)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{K-M}\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2}}\right),\quad\xi_{*}>2.

Gathering all the terms we have

(37)

\|\langle v\rangle^{4}G_{K}^{l}\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{K}{K-M}\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2}}\right),

where the constants is independent of
\epsilon\in[0,1]
. Finally we bound the second term, by Fubini’s theorem we have

\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2}))^{2}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2}))^{2}dx\right)dv.

Integrate (
25
) first in
x
and then in
t,v
gives

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2}))^{2}dx\right)dv

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1}))^{2}dxdv+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}\left(\tilde{Q}(Q,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l}\right)dvdxd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau_{1}/2}(\tilde{Q}(G,F)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau,

the last term can be estimated the same way as (
37
). Gathering all the terms we have

\displaystyle\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2})

(38)

\displaystyle+C_{l}\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau+C\frac{K}{K-M}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta_{i}}}{(K-M)^{a_{i}}},

where

\beta_{1}=\frac{1}{2}(1+r_{*}/p^{\prime}),\quad\beta_{2}=r_{*}/p,\quad\beta_{3}=\beta_{4}=r_{*},\quad a_{1}=(\xi-2p^{\prime})/(2p^{\prime}),\quad a_{2}=(\xi_{*}-2p)/p,\quad a_{3}=\xi_{*}-1,\quad a_{4}=\xi_{*}-2,

it’s easily seen that
\beta_{i}>1,a_{i}>0
for all
i=1,2,3,4
. For any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
, by Lemma
4.1
and (
33
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}=

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+g,\mu+f),f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\epsilon(L_{\alpha}(\mu+f),f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(2c_{1}-C_{l}\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{|\gamma|+9}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{l}(1+K)(1+\sup_{x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}}+\epsilon C_{l}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\epsilon C_{l}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-c_{1}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{l}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}}.

Integrate in
[T_{1},T_{2}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and similarly as (
34
), (
35
) and (
4
) we have

(39)

\displaystyle\|f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+c_{1}\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau

(40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+C_{l}(1+K)\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(\tau)\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}d\tau

(41)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\frac{K}{K-M}\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-1}}+\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{r_{*}}}{(K-M)^{\xi_{*}-2}}\right).

So the proof is ended by adding the two terms (
4
) and (
39
), then taking
C_{0}
large such that

\frac{C_{l}}{C_{0}}\leq\frac{c_{1}}{2}.

Since the estimates are all independent of
\epsilon
, the result is independent of
\epsilon
. Since
(-f)_{K,+}^{l}
satisfies the same estimate as
f_{K,+}^{l}
, the proof is the same and thus omitted.
∎

Before proving the
L^{\infty}
bound, we need a
L^{2}
bound on the zeroth level energy
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
defined by

(42)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(0,0,T):=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f_{+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dxd\tau

(43)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{1/p}.

where
f_{+}
denotes the positive part of
f
and

f_{+}^{l}:=\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{+}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Suppose
G=\mu+g,F=\mu+f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T>0,\alpha\geq 0
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
24
). Suppose in addition G satisfies that

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8,l\geq 3+2\alpha
, suppose

(44)

\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},\quad\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\leq C,

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then for any
0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}
, there exist
s"\in(0,s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+r})
and
p^{b}:=p^{b}(l,\gamma,s,s^{\prime})>1
such that for any
1<p<p^{b}
, we have

\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\left(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2j}T^{j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{j}\right),\quad p^{\prime}=p/(2-p).

The same estimate holds for
(-f)_{+}^{l}
and its associated
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
.

Proof.

By (
44
) we have
0\leq C(g)\leq 1+C
, taking supremum on
[0,T]
in Lemma
3.9
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|f_{+}^{l}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{+}^{l}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+c_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2}T+\epsilon^{2}T):=C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}D.

Let’s concentrate on the last term, for
p=(1,2),0<s"<\beta<s^{\prime}
, using Lemma
2.18
with
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p}
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\|(-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}^{p}dvd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{p}\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x}}^{\frac{p}{2}}+\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x}}^{p}dvd\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x,v}}^{p^{\prime}}d\tau\right)^{\frac{2-p}{2}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\int_{0}^{T}\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{x,v}}^{p^{\prime}}d\tau+\int_{0}^{T}\|(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau.

The control of the
L^{2p}
and
L^{2p^{\prime}}
norms of
f_{+}^{l}
are both through interpolations. First we have

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}\leq\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{1-\beta_{p}}\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{\xi(p)}_{x,v}}^{\beta_{p}},\quad\xi(p)=\frac{2}{2-p}>2,\quad\beta_{p}=\frac{1}{p}.

For any
\beta>0
, by choosing
\xi(p)=r(s,\beta,3)
in Lemma
2.16
we have

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{\xi(p)}_{x,v}}\lesssim\left(\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|f_{+}^{l}(x,\cdot)\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dx\right)^{1/2}+\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\|f_{+}^{l}(\cdot,v)\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{H^{\beta}_{x}}^{2}dv\right)^{1/2}.

Consequently, we have

(45)

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2p}\leq\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2(p-1)}(\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}f_{+}^{l}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}).

For any
q>1
we have

\displaystyle\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}f_{+}^{l}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{\eta\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2l-4+\gamma}\langle\eta\rangle^{2\beta}|\mathcal{F}_{x}(f_{+})|^{2}dv

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\sum_{\eta\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}\frac{1}{q}\langle v\rangle^{(2l-4+\gamma)q}+(1-\frac{1}{q})\langle\eta\rangle^{2\beta\frac{q}{q-1}}|\mathcal{F}_{x}(f_{+})|^{2}dv.

Taking
q=\frac{2l+\gamma}{2l+\gamma-4}
, if we assume
2\beta\frac{q}{q-1}<2s^{\prime}
or equivalently
\beta<s^{\prime}(1-\frac{1}{q})=s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+\gamma}
, then we have

\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq C_{l,\gamma}(\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}).

Integrate in
[0,T]
together with Lemma
3.9
we have

\int_{0}^{T}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{\beta/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\leq C_{l,\gamma}\int_{0}^{T}(\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2})d\tau\leq CD.

Since
\xi
is an increasing function in
\beta
, we obtain the corresponding range for
\xi(p)
and for
p
as

\xi(p)\in(2,r(s,s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+\gamma},3)):=(2,r^{b}),\quad p\in(1,2-2/r^{b}):=(1,p^{b}),

where
r(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in Lemma
2.16
. It is clear by its definition that
p^{b}
depends on
l,\gamma,s,s^{\prime}
. Using such parameters and combining (
45
), we obtain that

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2p}\leq C\|f_{+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2(p-1)}(\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}(f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}),

by Lemma
3.9
we have

(46)

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2p}\leq CD^{p},\quad p\in(1,p^{b}).

Taking
p^{\prime}
close to 1 such that
p^{\prime}\in(1,p^{b})
, (
46
) still holds when
p
is replaced by
p^{\prime}
, which means

\|f_{+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}\|_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{x,v}}^{2p^{\prime}}\leq CD^{p^{\prime}}.

The same estimate holds for
(-f)_{+}^{l}
and its associated
E_{0}
since Lemma
3.9
applies to the absolute value of
f
which contains both positive and negative parts of
f
. So the theorem is thus proved.
∎

Theorem 4.7
.

Suppose
G=\mu+g,F=\mu+f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T>0,\alpha\geq 0
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
24
). Suppose in addition G satisfies that

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8,l\geq 3+2\alpha
, suppose

\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},\quad\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\leq C,

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small. Assume that the initial data satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<+\infty.

Then there exist a constant
l_{0}\geq 0
which depends on
s,\gamma,l
such that if we additionally suppose that

\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+l_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{1}<+\infty,

for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Then it follows that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\max\{2\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},K_{0}^{1}\},

where

K_{0}^{1}:=C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2j}T^{j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{j})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}},\quad p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p},

and
a_{i},\beta_{i}
is defined in Lemma
4.6
.

Proof.

Choose
(p,s^{\prime\prime})
close enough to
(1,0)
such that

s^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{(2l+4)(s+3)},\quad p=\frac{\min\{p^{a},p^{b}\}}{2}+\frac{1}{2},

thus
(p,s^{\prime\prime})
satisfies

\quad 0<s^{\prime\prime}<s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+4},\quad 0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)},\quad 1<p<\min\{p^{a},p^{b}\},

where
p^{a}
and
p^{b}
is defined in Lemma
4.5
and Lemma
4.6
respectively. Such choice
p,s^{\prime\prime}
guarantees Lemma
4.5
and
4.6
hold. We use a classical iteration scheme to prove the estimate for the
L^{\infty}
norm for the solution. Fix
K_{0}
to be determined, we introduce an increasing level set
M_{k}
as

M_{k}:=K_{0}(1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}),\quad k=0,1,2....

Take
T_{2}\in[0,T]
with
T>0
fixed in the analysis. In order to simplify the notation, denote

f_{k}:=f_{M_{k},+}^{l},\quad\mathcal{E}_{k}:=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k},0,T),\quad k=0,1,2...,

choose
M=M_{k-1}<M_{k}=K
and
T_{1}=0
in Lemma
4.5
and by

\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k-1},0,T_{2})\leq\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k-1},0,T)=\mathcal{E}_{k-1},\quad k=1,2...,

together with Lemma
4.5
, for any
0\leq T_{2}\leq T
we have

\displaystyle\|f_{k}(T_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f_{k}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{0}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{{s^{\prime\prime}}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(f_{k})^{2})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{CK}{K-M}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,0,T_{2})^{\beta_{i}}}{(K-M)^{a_{i}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}},

where in the last line we have used
K-M=M_{k}-M_{k-1}=K_{0}(\frac{1}{2^{k-1}}-\frac{1}{2^{k}})=K_{0}\frac{1}{2^{k}},K\leq K_{0}
. Taking supremum in
T_{2}\in[0,T]
we deduce

\mathcal{E}_{k}\leq C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(0)\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}.

By taking
K_{0}\geq 2\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
, we have
f_{k}(0)=0,k=1,2,...
, hence

\mathcal{E}_{k}\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}.

Let

Q_{0}=\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}(2^{\frac{a_{i}+1}{\beta_{i}-1}}),\quad\mathcal{E}_{K}^{*}=\mathcal{E}_{0}(1/Q_{0})^{k},\quad k=0,1,2,...,

since
\beta_{i}>1,\alpha_{i}>0
we have
Q_{0}>1
. Suppose

K_{0}\geq K_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0}):=\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\{4^{\frac{1}{a_{i}}}C^{\frac{1}{a_{i}}}\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}}Q_{0}^{\frac{\beta_{i}}{a_{i}}}\}.

We easily compute

\mathcal{E}_{0}^{*}=\mathcal{E}_{0},\quad C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}(\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{*})^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\beta_{i}}Q_{0}^{-\beta_{i}(k-1)}}{4\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\beta_{i}-1}Q_{0}^{\beta_{i}}}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{Q_{0}^{k(\beta_{i}-1)}\mathcal{E}_{0}}{4Q_{0}^{\beta_{i}k}}\leq\mathcal{E}_{0}(1/Q_{0})^{k}=\mathcal{E}_{k}^{*}.

by comparison principle we have
\mathcal{E}_{k}\leq\mathcal{E}_{k}^{*}\to 0
, as
k\to\infty
. In particular we deduce

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|f_{K_{0},+}^{l}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}=0,\quad\hbox{for}\quad K_{0}=\max\{2\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},K_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\}.

which implies that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{+}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq K_{0},

By Lemma
4.6
we have

K_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2j}T^{j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{j})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}}:=K_{0}^{1},\quad p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p}.

A similar bound is also valid for
-f
since Lemma
4.5
and Lemma
4.6
have their corresponding
-f
version. The proof is thus ended. ∎

Remark 4.8
.

For any
s,\gamma
given, since
l_{0}
depends on
l
, in the following we will use
l_{0}(l)
to denote its dependence.

5.
Local existence for the linearized equation

In this section we establish the local existence of a modified linearized Boltzmann equation. The ambient space for contraction and the subset
H_{k}
is defined by

X_{k}:=L^{\infty}(0,T,L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})),\quad H_{k}:=\{g\in X_{k}|\mu+g\geq 0\},

for some constant
k\geq 0
. The precise equation consider in this section is

(47)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),f)+Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),\mu),\quad f(0,x,v)=f_{0}(x,v)

where we recall
\chi\in C^{\infty}
is defined in (
7
) satisfies
0\leq\chi\leq 1
,
|\nabla\chi|\leq 4/\delta_{0}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{\chi}(x):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad|x|\leq\delta_{0}\\
&0,\quad|x|\geq 2\delta_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

Note that since
g\in H_{k}
, we have
\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)\geq 0
. Before going to the proof of the local existence, we first prove a lemma on the cutoff function
\chi
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For any smooth function
g,h
, for any constant
k_{0}\geq 0
, there exist a constant
C
independent of
\delta_{0},k_{0},g,h
such that

|g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h)|\leq C|g-h|.

Proof.

Denote

p:=\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g,\quad q:=\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h.

we only need to prove that

|p\chi(p)-q\chi(q)|\leq C|p-q|,\quad\forall p,q\in{\mathbb{R}}.

We split into three cases. If
|p|\geq 3\delta_{0}
and
|q|\geq 2\delta_{0}
we have

|p\chi(p)-q\chi(q)|=0\leq|p-q|.

If
|p|\geq 3\delta_{0}
and
|q|\leq 2\delta_{0}
we have

|p\chi(p)-q\chi(q)|=|q\chi(q)|\leq|q|\leq 2\delta_{0}\leq 2|p-q|.

It remains to prove the case
|p|\leq 3\delta_{0}
. Since
|\chi|\leq 1
we have

|p\chi(p)-q\chi(q)|=|p(\chi(p)-\chi(q))+\chi(q)(p-q)|\leq|p||\chi(p)-\chi(q)|+|\chi(q)||p-q|\leq|p||\chi(p)-\chi(q)|+|p-q|,

since
|\nabla\chi|\leq 4/\delta_{0}
we deduce

|p||\chi(p)-\chi(q)|+|p-q|\leq 12|p-q|+|p-q|\leq 13|p-q|.

Gathering all the cases the lemma is thus proved.
∎

The main theorem for the linearized equation is

Lemma 5.2
.

Suppose
g,f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
47
). Let
g\in H_{k}
and let
\chi
be the cutoff function defined in (
7
).

(1) Let
T\geq 0
be arbitrary but fixed. Suppose the initial data
f_{0}\in H_{k}
and assume that
k_{0}\geq k+8
and
k\geq 12
, suppose that
\delta_{0}
is small enough, the solution (
47
) has a unique solution
f\in H_{k}
.

(2) In addition, if we assume further that
k\geq l_{0}(14)+14,\alpha=5
, where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
4.7
. Then there exist constants
\delta_{1},\epsilon_{*}>0
and
T_{\delta_{0}}\in[0,1]
which is independent of
\epsilon
such that if the initial data satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},

then for any
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{*},T\in[0,T_{\delta_{0}}]
, the solution obtained in (1) satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0}.

The choice of
\epsilon_{*},\delta_{*}
only depends on
r,s,k_{0},\delta_{0}
.

Proof.

Denote

Th:=-\partial_{t}h-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h-\epsilon L_{\alpha}h-Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),h).

Let
S
be the space of test function given by

S=C^{\infty}_{0}((-\infty,T],C^{\infty}({\mathbb{T}}^{3},C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))).

Let
h\in S
, multiply
Th
by
h\langle v\rangle^{2k}
and integrate in
x,v
we have

\displaystyle(Th,h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}+\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2k}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}h-\nabla_{v}\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\cdot\nabla_{v}h)hdvdx

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),h)h\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx.

First we easily compute

\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2k}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}h-\nabla_{v}\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\cdot\nabla_{v}h)hdvdx\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\alpha}}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}\nabla_{v}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}dxdv-C_{k}\epsilon\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

Denote

T_{0}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),h)h\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx.

It’s easily seen that if
\delta_{0}>0
is small,
G=\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)
satisfies

G\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. If
\delta_{0}>0
is small enough, by Theorem
3.4
we have

\displaystyle(Q(G,f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle-\gamma_{2}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}-\gamma_{1}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k+5+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|g\chi\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(\gamma_{2}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}-(\gamma_{1}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2},

for some constant
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},C_{k}>0
. Gathering the terms we easily deduce that

(Th,h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\geq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}-C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

By Grönwall’s inequality we have

\int_{t}^{T}e^{2C_{k}\tau}(Th,h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau\geq\frac{1}{2}e^{2C_{k}t}\|h(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2},\quad\forall t\in[0,T],

and we easily have

\int_{t}^{T}e^{2C_{k}\tau}(Th,h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\int_{0}^{T}e^{2C_{k}\tau}\|Th\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau,\quad\forall t\in[0,T],

which implies

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}h\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{T,k}\int_{0}^{T}\|Th\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau,\quad\forall t\in[0,T].

Denote

W:=TS=\{w|w=Th,h\in S\},\quad Y=L^{1}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})),\quad X=Y^{*}=L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})),

where the adjoint is taken in the weighted space
L^{2}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Then
W
is a subspace of
Y
and we have already shown that

\|h\|_{X}\leq C_{T,k}\|w\|_{Y}.

Denote

R_{\epsilon}=-\epsilon(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\mu-\nabla_{v}\cdot(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v})\mu),

and define the linear mapping on
W
by

G(w)=(h_{0},f_{0})_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}+\int_{0}^{T}(Q(g\chi,\mu),h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau+\int_{0}^{T}(h,R_{\epsilon})_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau.

It is easily seen that

(h_{0},f_{0})_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\leq\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k})}\leq C_{T,k}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|w\|_{Y}.

For the second term we easily deduce

\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2k}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\mu-\nabla_{v}\cdot(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v})\mu)hdvdx\leq C_{k}\epsilon\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\leq C_{k}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}},

which implies

\int_{0}^{T}(h,R_{\epsilon})_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau\leq C_{T,k}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k})}\leq C_{T,k}\|w\|_{Y}.

Since
k_{0}-8\geq k
, by Lemma
2.3
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}(Q(g\chi,\mu),h)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}(Q(g\chi,\mu),h\langle v\rangle^{2k})dxd\tau\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|g\|_{L^{1}_{k+\gamma+2s}\cap L^{2}_{3}}\|h\|_{L^{2}_{k}}dxd\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T,k}\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{T,k}\sup_{t,x}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\|w\|_{Y}.

This shows that
G
is a bounded linear functional on
W
, thus can be extended to
Y
, by Hahn-Banach theorem, there exists
f\in X
such that

G(w)=\int_{0}^{T}(w,f)_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}d\tau,

with

\|f\|_{X}\leq C_{T,k}\|f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}+C_{T,k}(1+\sup_{t,x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}).

so the existence is thus proved. To show that
f\in H_{k}
, we need to prove that
\mu+f\geq 0
. Let
F=\mu+f
and
G=\mu+g\chi
, then
G\geq 0
and
F
satisfies

(48)

\partial_{t}F+v\cdot\nabla_{x}F=-\epsilon(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}I-\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}))F+Q(G,F).

Denote
\eta(x)=\frac{1}{2}(x_{-})^{2},x_{-}=\min\{x,0\},F_{\pm}=\pm\max\{\pm F,0\}
. Multiply (
48
) by
\langle v\rangle^{2k}F_{-}
we have

\frac{1}{2}\langle v\rangle^{2k}(\partial_{t}(F_{-})^{2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(F_{-})^{2})=-\epsilon\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha+2k}(FF_{-})+\epsilon\langle v\rangle^{2k}F_{-}\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}))F+Q(G,F)F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}.

We easily compute

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}-\epsilon\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha+2k}(FF_{-})dvdx=-\epsilon\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2},

Since
\eta^{\prime\prime}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2k}F_{-}\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}F)dvdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2k}F_{-})\cdot(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}F)dvdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha+2k}\eta^{\prime\prime}(F)|\nabla_{v}F|^{2}dvdx-\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{-}\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2k})\cdot(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}F)dvdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha+2k}\eta^{\prime\prime}(F)|\nabla_{v}F|^{2}dvdx+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}F_{-}^{2}\nabla_{v}(\langle v\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla_{v}\langle v\rangle^{2k})dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\epsilon\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k-1}F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}+C_{k}\epsilon\|\langle v\rangle^{k}F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}^{2}.

Since
F_{+}F_{-}=0,F_{+}\geq 0,F_{-}\leq 0,G\geq 0
, by Theorem
3.4
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F)F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F_{-})F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F_{+})F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F_{-})F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}BG_{*}^{\prime}(F_{+})^{\prime}F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}BG_{*}F_{+}F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(G,F_{-})F_{-}\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(\gamma_{2}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}-(\gamma_{1}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

Gathering all the terms we have

\frac{d}{dt}\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}\leq C_{k}\|F_{-}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2},\quad F_{-}|_{t=0}=0,

then by Grönwall’s Lemma we conclude that
F_{-}=0
, which implies
f\in H_{k}
. Uniqueness also follows by Lemma
3.9
, thus the proof for (1) is finished. By (
3
) we have

\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq C_{k}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{k},

so for
T\in[0,1]
we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq C_{k}(\|\langle v\rangle^{k}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+1)<+\infty.

Since
\alpha=5
, we have
3+2\alpha\leq 14
, together with
k-l_{0}(14)\geq 14
, take
l=14
in Theorem
4.7
we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\max\{2\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},K_{0}^{1}\},

where

K_{0}^{1}:=C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\sup_{t,x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}^{2j}T^{j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{j})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}},\quad p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p},

if
T\leq 1
, we easily conclude by taking
\delta_{1},T_{\delta_{0}},\epsilon_{*}
small.
∎

6.
Nonlinear local theory for weak singularity

In this section we are considering the

(49)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f),\mu+f),\quad f(0,x,v)=f_{0}(x,v)

recall the cutoff function
\chi\in C^{\infty}
is defined in (
7
).
If the solution to (
49
) satisfies

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0},

the solution becomes a solution to

(50)

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+f,\mu+f),\quad f(0,x,v)=f_{0}(x,v)

We first prove the existence of the localized equation.

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose
g,f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1/2],\gamma+2s>-1,\alpha=5
. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
49
). Suppose
k_{0}
and the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies

k_{0}-l_{0}(14)\geq 14+8,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14+l_{0}(14)}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},

where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
4.7
. For any
\delta_{0}>0
small satisfies the same assumptions in the lemma above, there exist
\delta_{1},\epsilon_{*}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{*}]
, there exist a time
T_{\epsilon}
such that (
49
) has a solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{14}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Moreover the solution
f
satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0}.

where
\delta_{1}
,
\epsilon
is defined in Lemma
5.2
.

Proof.

In this proof we will set
k=14
. For any
g\in H_{k}
, define the map

\Gamma:H_{k}\to H_{k},\quad\Gamma g=f,

where
f
is the unique solution to the equation

\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)+Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),f)+Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),\mu),\quad f(0)=f_{0}.

Since
f_{0}\in H_{14}=H_{k}
, Lemma
5.2
guarantees that
\Gamma
is well-defined provided
\delta_{0},\epsilon,T
are small enough. Our goal is to show that
\Gamma
is a contraction map on
L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Let
g,h\in H_{k}
and let
f_{g},g_{h}
be the corresponding solution such that

\partial_{t}f_{g}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{g}=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f_{g})+Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),f_{g})+Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),\mu),\quad f_{g}(0)=f_{0},

and

\partial_{t}f_{h}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{h}=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f_{h})+Q(\mu+h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),f_{h})+Q(h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),\mu),\quad f_{g}(0)=f_{0}.

Since
f_{0}\in H_{14+l_{0}(14)}
, by Lemma
5.2
we have there exist a
T_{\delta_{0}}
such that for all
T\in[0,T_{\delta_{0}}]
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0}.

The difference of the two equations satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(f_{g}-f_{h})+v\cdot\nabla_{x}(f_{g}-f_{h})=

\displaystyle\epsilon L_{\alpha}(f_{g}-f_{h})+Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),f_{g}-f_{h})

(51)

\displaystyle+Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),f_{h})+Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),\mu).

In the following we will multiply both side of (
6
) by
(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}
and integrate. For the first term in the right hand side we easily compute

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\epsilon L_{\alpha}(f_{g}-f_{h})(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{k}\epsilon\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

By Theorem
3.4
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g),f_{g}-f_{h})(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-(\gamma_{2}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}-(\gamma_{1}-C_{k}\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}})\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k+\gamma/2-s^{\prime}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

Since
k=14,\alpha=5,s\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
we have

\gamma+2s+k-\alpha+4\leq 14,\quad\gamma+2s+k-\alpha+2\leq k,\quad 2s\leq 1.

together with Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
5.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),f_{h})(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h)\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+2s+k-\alpha}\cap L^{2}_{2}}\|f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma+2s+k-\alpha}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{H^{2s}_{k+\alpha}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sup_{x}\|f_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\gamma+2s+k-\alpha+4}})\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{H^{2s}_{k+\alpha}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{\epsilon}\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

By Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
5.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),\mu)(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{k,\epsilon}\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

Combining the terms above we have

\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}\leq C_{k,\epsilon}\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}+C_{k,\epsilon}\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2}.

Since
f_{g}(0)=f_{h}(0)=f_{0}
, choosing
T
small enough which may depend on
\epsilon
, by Grönwall’s inequality we have

\|f_{g}-f_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k})}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|g-h\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k})}^{2},

which implies
\Gamma
is a contraction map. We obtain a solution to (
47
) by fixed point theorem. The
L^{\infty}
bound is a direct consequence of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

Now we briefly describe how the discussions in Section
4
still works for (
49
). First replacing
g
in Lemma
4.5
by
f\chi
, we have

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose
F=\mu+f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T>0,\alpha\geq 0
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
49
). Suppose in addition
F
satisfies that

F\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|F\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|F\|_{L\log L})<B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}
, suppose

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then there exists
s">0
and
p^{a}>0
such that for any
p\in(1,p^{a})
there exist
l_{0}>0
which depends on
s^{\prime\prime},p
such that, if we assume

\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l_{0}+l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C_{1}<+\infty,

Then for any
0\leq T_{1}\leq T_{2}\leq T,\epsilon\in(0,1),0\leq M<K
we have

\displaystyle\|f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{T_{1}}^{T_{2}}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{{s^{\prime\prime}}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-4+\gamma}(f_{K,+}^{l})^{2})\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}d\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{K,+}^{l}(T_{1})\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{CK}{K-M}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M,T_{1},T_{2})^{\beta_{i}}}{(K-M)^{a_{i}}},

for some constant
\beta_{i}>1,a_{i}>0
are defined later. The constant
C
is independent of
\epsilon,K,M,f,T_{1},T_{2}
. Furthermore, the same estimate holds for
h=-f
with
f_{K,+}^{l}
replaced by
h_{K,+}^{l}
.

For Lemma
4.6
, replacing the use of Lemma
3.9
by Corollary
3.10
, and repeating the proof of Lemma
4.6
we have

Lemma 6.3
.

Suppose
f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T>0,\alpha\geq 0
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
49
). Suppose in addition G satisfies that

\mu+f\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f)\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A>0,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f)\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f)\|_{L\log L})\leq B<+\infty,

for some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0},l\geq 3+2\alpha
, suppose

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small. Then for any
0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}
, there exist
s"\in(0,s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+r})
and
p^{b}:=p^{b}(l,\gamma,s,s^{\prime})>1
such that for any
1<p<p^{b}
, we have

\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l}T}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\left(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{j}\right),\quad p^{\prime}=p/(2-p),

where
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
is defined in (
42
). The same estimate holds for
(-f)_{+}^{l}
and its associated
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
.

To pass the limit in
\epsilon
we need to show that the time interval of existence is independent of
\epsilon
. So we need to find the relation between the smallness of the initial data and the smallness of the solution.

Lemma 6.4
.

Suppose
f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T\in[0,1],\alpha=5
be fixed. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
49
). Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
14\leq k_{0}
, suppose

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\leq\delta_{0},

for some constant
\delta_{0}>0
small, where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
4.7
. Then it follows that

(52)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\max\{2\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},K_{0}^{1}\},

where

K_{0}^{1}:=C_{k_{0}}e^{C_{k_{0}}T}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}(\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+\epsilon^{2j}T^{2j})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}},\quad p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p},

Moreover, for any
0\leq T\leq 1
, there exist two constants
\delta_{*},\epsilon_{*}>0
small enough which is independent of
T
such that if we assume

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{*},

then we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\leq\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}<\frac{\delta_{0}}{2},

where
\delta_{1}
is defined in Lemma
5.2
.

Proof.

Take
l=k_{0}
in Lemma
6.2
and
6.3
above,
k_{0}
satisfies the requirements in Lemma
6.2
and
6.3
. We just need to check that the assumptions in Lemma
6.2
and
6.3
holds. First we have

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7+|\gamma|}}\leq C\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{14}}\leq\delta_{0}\quad,\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{9+|\gamma|}}\leq C\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{14}}\leq\delta_{0},

and

\inf_{t,x}\|\mu+f\chi\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq\|\mu\|_{L^{1}_{v}}-\|\langle v\rangle^{-4}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\|\langle v\rangle^{4}f\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\geq 1-C\delta_{0}\geq A>0,

for some constant
A>0
, also we have

\sup_{t,x}\|\mu+f\chi\|_{L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L}\leq\sup_{t,x}\|\mu\|_{L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L}+\sup_{t,x}\|f\chi\|_{L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L}\leq C_{0}(1+\delta_{0})\leq B,

for some constant
B>0
. By Corollary
3.10
apply for
l=k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})
we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq Ce^{C_{l}T}(1+\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2})<+\infty,

Note that to make Corollary
3.10
works, we only need

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},

this weight is independent of
k_{0}
.
Then by the same proof as Theorem
4.7
, (
52
) is thus proved. Since
T\leq 1,\epsilon_{*}\leq 1,\delta_{*}<1,2/p>1,2p^{\prime}/p>1
, we have

K_{0}^{1}\leq C_{k_{0}}e^{C_{k_{0}}}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}(\delta_{*}+\epsilon_{*})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}}=C_{k_{0}}e^{C_{k_{0}}}(\delta_{*}+\epsilon_{*})^{\eta_{0}},\quad\eta_{0}=\min_{1\leq i\leq 4}\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}},

by setting

\delta_{*}=\min\{\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1},\frac{1}{2(2C_{k_{0}}e^{C_{k_{0}}})^{1/\eta_{0}}}\delta_{1}^{\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}}\},\quad\epsilon_{*}=\frac{1}{2(2C_{k_{0}}e^{C_{k_{0}}})^{1/\eta_{0}}}\delta_{1}^{\frac{1}{\eta_{0}}},

the lemma is thus proved.
∎

We summarize the above results to deduce the local existence for the regularized equation (
50
).

Theorem 6.5
.

Suppose
f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1/2],\gamma+2s>-1
. Let
T\in[0,1],\alpha=5
be fixed. Suppose
k_{0}\geq l_{0}(14)+22
, where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
4.7
. Suppose

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<\delta_{*},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,

suppose
\delta_{*},\epsilon_{*}
is defined in Lemma
6.4
. then for any
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{*}
the equation (
49
) has a solution
f
satisfying

(53)

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}<\delta_{0}.

In particular, the solution
f
