becomes a solution to (
50
).

Proof.

Since
k_{0}\geq l_{0}(14)+22
, by Lemma
6.1
we have, there exist a
T_{\epsilon}>0
such that (
50
) has a solution satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0},

and Lemma
6.4
implies that

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}.

We claim that such
T_{\epsilon}
can be extended to
T
independent of
\epsilon
. Indeed by Lemma
6.1
and the Lemma
6.4
we first extend
T_{\epsilon}
to
\tilde{T}_{\epsilon}
, where
T_{\epsilon}
is the largest interval such that

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\tilde{T}_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1}.

Since
k_{0}\geq l_{0}(20)+28
, so for any
t<\tilde{T}_{\epsilon}
we can apply Lemma
6.1
on
f_{0}=f(t)
to continue extension. Since the estimates in Corollary
3.10
and Lemma
6.4
are all independent of
\epsilon
, we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\tilde{T}_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{1},

so
\tilde{T}_{\epsilon}
can be continued to the maximum interval
[0,T]
for any
T\leq 1
. Since (
53
) implies
\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f)=1
, the solution
f
becomes a solution to (
50
).

∎

Since we have obtained a solution to (
50
), we are ready to pass the limit in
\epsilon
and obtain a local solution the Boltzmann equation (
1
).

Theorem 6.6
.

Suppose that
s\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
and
k_{0}\geq l_{0}(14)+22
,
l_{0}
is defined Lemma
3.9
. Suppose that
f_{0}
satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<\delta_{*},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,

where
\delta_{*}
is defined in Lemma
6.4
and
\delta_{0}
satisfies all the bound above. Then for any
T\leq 1
, the nonlinear Boltzmann equation (
1
) has a solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})})
. Moreover,
f
satisfies the bound

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{\delta_{1}}{2}<\delta_{0}.

Proof.

Denote
f^{\epsilon}
as the local solution to (
50
). By Corollary
3.9
, Lemma
5.2
and Lemma
6.4
we obtain the uniform in
\epsilon
bound of
f^{\epsilon}
in the following space

L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\cap H^{s^{\prime}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3},H^{s}_{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})),\quad s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}.

We can extract a subsequence, still denote
f^{\epsilon}
such that

\|f^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{\delta_{1}}{2},

by the uniform polynomial decay and a diagonal argument, we have

f^{\epsilon}\to f\quad\hbox{strongly in}\quad L^{2}_{t,x}L^{2}_{l_{0}(k_{0})+k_{0}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}),

such strong convergence implies the convergence of
Q(f_{\epsilon},f_{\epsilon})
to
Q(f,f)
as distributions. Indeed taking
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
as test functions, by Lemma
2.15
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(f^{\epsilon},f^{\epsilon})\phi(v)dv-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q(f,f)\phi(v)dv\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(f^{\epsilon}(v_{*})f^{\epsilon}(v)-f(v_{*})f(v))(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|f^{\epsilon}(v_{*})f^{\epsilon}(v)-f(v_{*})f(v)||v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))d\sigma\right|dvdv_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f^{\epsilon}(v_{*})f^{\epsilon}(v)-f(v_{*})f(v)|dvdv_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f^{\epsilon}(v_{*})-f(v_{*})||f^{\epsilon}(v)|dvdv_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle+\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\left\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f^{\epsilon}(v)-f(v)||f(v_{*})|dvdv_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}(\sup_{t,x}\|f_{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{6}})\|f^{\epsilon}-f\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}L^{2}_{4}}\to 0\quad\hbox{as}\quad\epsilon\to 0.

Therefore we obtain a solution
f
to the Boltzmann equation, where
f
lives in the space

L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))\cap H^{s^{\prime}}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3},H^{s}_{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})),\quad s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)},

so the theorem is ended.
∎

7.
Global existence

We recall that
L
denotes for the linearized operator

Lf=Q(\mu,f)+Q(f,\mu)-v\cdot\nabla_{x}f,

and the nonlinear Boltzmann equation is recast as

\partial_{t}f=Lf+Q(f,f),\quad(t,x,v)\in(0,T)\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}.

Lemma 7.1
.

(
[
11
]
, Lemma 3.4 + Lemma
2.3
) For any
k\geq 12
, for any smooth function
f,g
, denote
X_{k}=L^{2}_{k},Y_{k}=H^{s}_{k+\gamma/2},Z_{k}=H^{-s}_{k-\gamma/2}
which is the dual of
Y_{k}
respect to
X_{k}
, then we have

\|Q(f,g)\|_{Z_{k}}\lesssim\|f\|_{X_{k}}\|g\|_{Y_{k+2}}+\|f\|_{Y_{k}}\|g\|_{X_{k}},

which implies

\|Q(f,f)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{k-\gamma/2}}\lesssim(\sup_{x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{k}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{k+2-\gamma}}.

Lemma 7.2
.

(
[
11
]
, Corollary 3.3, Lemma 5.2)
Let
f
smooth be the solution to the linearized Boltzmann equation

\partial_{t}f=Lf,

Then if
\gamma<0
, then for any
k\geq 12
large we have

\|S_{L}(t)f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim\theta(t)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}},\quad\|S_{L}(t)f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\lesssim t^{-1/2}\theta(t)\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{-s}_{k-\gamma/2}},

where
\theta(t)=e^{-\lambda t}
for some
\lambda>0
if
\gamma=0
,
\theta(t)=(1+t)^{-\frac{|k-12|}{|\gamma|}}
if
\gamma\in(-3,0)
.

Theorem 7.3
.

Suppose
f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Suppose that
F=\mu+f\geq 0
is a solution of the Boltzmann equation (
1
). For any
l\geq 20
, assume that

\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7+|\gamma|}}\leq\delta_{0},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty.

for some small constant
\delta_{0}>0
. Denote
X=L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{l},Y=L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}
. Define the norm
|||f|||_{X}
and the associate scalar product on
\Pi X
by

|||f|||_{X}^{2}=\eta\|f\|_{X}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\infty}\|S_{L}(\tau)f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau,\quad((f,g))_{X}=\eta(f,g)_{X}+\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)f,S_{L}(\tau)g)_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau.

Then there exist some
\eta>0
, such that the norm
|||\cdot|||
is equivalent to
\|\cdot\|_{X}
on
\Pi X
, moreover there exist some constant
K>0
such that any smooth solution to the Boltzmann equation with
Pf_{0}=0
satisfies

(54)

\frac{d}{dt}|||f|||_{X}^{2}\leq-K\|f\|_{Y}^{2}.

As a consequence if
\gamma=0
, we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\quad t\in[0,T],

for some constant
C,\lambda>0
. If
\gamma\in(-3,0)
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{l_{1}}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq Ct^{-\frac{|l-14|}{|\gamma|}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\quad t\in[0,T],\quad\forall 14\leq l_{1}<l.

for some constant
C>0
. Moreover for any
0<s^{\prime}<\frac{s}{2(s+3)}
, we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\gamma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq C\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

where all the constants
C
are all independent of
T
.

Proof.

First by Lemma
7.2

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|S_{L}(\tau)f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim\|f\|_{X}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\theta^{2}(\tau)d\tau\lesssim\|f\|_{X}^{2}.

The equivalence between two norms is thus proved. Then we easily compute

\frac{d}{dt}|||f(t)|||^{2}_{X}=\eta(Q(\mu+f,\mu+f),f)_{X}+\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Lf,S_{L}(\tau)f)_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau+\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Q(f,f),S_{L}(\tau)f)_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau.

We will estimate the terms separately, first by Theorem
3.6
we have

\displaystyle(Q(\mu+f,\mu+f),f)_{X}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}(Q(\mu+f,\mu+f),f\langle v\rangle^{2k})dx\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}-2c_{0}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{|\gamma|+7}}\|f\|_{H^{s}_{l+\gamma/2}}^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\|f\|_{Y}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{k}\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7+|\gamma|}}\|f\|_{Y}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-c_{0}\|f\|_{Y}^{2}+C_{k}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

For the second term, by Lemma
7.2
, we have

\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Lf,S_{L}(\tau)f)_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{d}{d\tau}\|S_{L}(\tau)f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau=\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\|S_{L}(\tau)f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}-\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}=-\|f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

For the last term, by Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.2
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(S_{L}(\tau)Q(f,f),S_{L}(\tau)f)_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|S_{L}(\tau)Q(f,f)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|S_{L}(\tau)f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|Q(f,f)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{-s}_{10-\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{10}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\theta_{1}(\tau)\theta(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{10}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{10+2-\gamma/2}}\|f\|_{Y}\lesssim\delta_{0}\|f\|_{Y}^{2}.

Combining all the terms and taking a suitable
\eta>0
small, (
54
) is thus proved. For the convergence rate, the case
\gamma\geq 0
can be proved directly by using Grönwall’s Lemma and the case
\gamma\in(-3,0)
can be proved by interpolation, see
[
10
]
Lemma 3.12 for exmaple. By integrate on
[0,T]
in (
54
) we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+K\int_{0}^{T}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\gamma/2}(1-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\leq C\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

where the constant
C
does not depend on
T
. By Lemma
2.3
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}({Q}(F,F))\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-s}({Q}(\mu+f,f)+Q(f,\mu))\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}}+\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}},

which implies

\int_{0}^{T}\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}({Q}(F,F))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim(1+\sup_{t,x}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{7}})\int_{0}^{T}\|\langle v\rangle^{7}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},

similarly as (
3
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{3}f(0)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle^{3}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{v})^{s/2}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}({Q}(F,F))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\langle v\rangle^{l_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{3}(I-\Delta_{v})^{-1}({Q}(F,F))\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau,

so the theorem is thus proved.
∎

Theorem 7.4
.

(Global Existence) Suppose
f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(0,1),\gamma+2s>-1
. Suppose that
F=\mu+f\geq 0
is a solution of the Boltzmann equation (
1
). Then for any
k_{0}\geq 14
there exist
\delta_{*},\delta_{0}>0
small such that if the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies

u+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{*},\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}+l_{0}(k_{0})}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad Pf_{0}=0.

Then the Boltzmann equation has a global solution satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,\infty)\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}.

Moreover, if
\gamma=0
, we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq Ce^{-\lambda t}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\quad\forall t\in[0,+\infty),

for some constant
C,\lambda>0
. If
\gamma\in(-3,0)
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{1}}f(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq Ct^{-\frac{|k_{0}-14|}{|\gamma|}}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}},\quad\forall t\in[0,+\infty),\quad\forall 14\leq k_{1}<k_{0}.

Proof.

The reason that Theorem
6.5
can only treat a short time existence is because that the bound of
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
relies on
T
(precisely
Ce^{CT}
), which will exceed if
T
is large (see Lemma
6.3
). Now by Theorem
7.3
, we know that the term
Ce^{CT}
can be replaced by a constant
C
which does not depend on
t
, so the result in Lemma
6.3
can be improved to

\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}\leq C_{k_{0}}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}\leq C_{k_{0}}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

As a consequence, there exists
C_{k_{0}}
independent of
T
such that

K_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}})\leq C_{k_{0}}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{\eta_{0}},\quad\eta_{0}=\min_{1\leq i\leq 4}\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}.

Taking
\delta_{*}
small, we conclude

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T)\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{2}<\delta_{0},\quad\forall T>0,

thus for any
T>0
, the equation can be extended beyond
T
, the global existence is thus proved. The convergence rate follows by Theorem
7.3
.
∎

Theorem 7.5
.

Suppose
k_{0}
and the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies the same condition as Theorem
7.4
above. Then there exist
C_{k_{0}},\eta_{0}
such that for any
t>1
the solution obtained Theorem
7.4
satisfies if
\gamma=0

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C_{k_{0}}e^{-\lambda t}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2\eta_{0}/p},

for some constant
\lambda>0
and if
\gamma\in(-3,0)

\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C_{k_{0}}t^{-\alpha}\|\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2\eta_{0}/p},

for some constant
\alpha>0
.

Proof.

For any
K,t_{1}>0
, let
\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(K,t_{1},+\infty)
be the energy functional defined in (
4
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(K,t_{1},+\infty):=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq t_{1}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{t_{1}}^{+\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dxd\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{t_{1}}^{+\infty}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{-2+\gamma/2}f_{K,+}^{l})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}\right)^{1/p}.

Our main goal is to remove the dependence of
L^{\infty}
norm of
f_{0}
so that we can have a decay in the weighted
L^{\infty}
norm, define the levels

M_{k}:=K_{0}(1-1/2^{k}),\quad k=0,1,2,...,

Setting
f_{k}=f_{M_{k},+}^{l}
and proceeding as Theorem
4.7
, we arrive at

(55)

\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k},t_{1},+\infty)\leq C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(t_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(t_{1})\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k-1},t_{1},+\infty)^{\beta_{i}},

for
k=1,2,...
. The parameters
a_{i}>0
,
\beta_{i}>0
are the same as Theorem
4.7
. Fix
T>0
and
T_{k}
be the increasing time sequence and denote
E_{k}
by

T_{k-1}:=T(1-\frac{1}{2^{k}}),\quad k=0,1,2...,\quad\mathcal{E}_{k}:=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k},T_{k},+\infty).

Using the monotonicity
\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)
in its first and second variables and integrate (
55
) in
t_{1}\in[T_{k-1},T_{k}]
,we deduce

\mathcal{E}_{k}=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k},T_{k},+\infty)\leq(T_{k}-T_{k-1})^{-1}\left(\int_{T_{k-1}}^{T_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau+\int_{T_{k-1}}^{T_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(\tau)\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\right)+C\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{K-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}}.

Similarly as (
34
) we have

\int_{T_{k-1}}^{T_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau\leq C\frac{\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(M_{k-1},T_{k-1},T_{k})^{r_{*}}}{(M_{k}-M_{k-1})^{\xi_{*}-2}}\leq C_{0}\frac{2^{k(\xi_{*}-2)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{r*}}{K_{0}^{\xi_{*}-2}}.

By Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
4.4
we have

\displaystyle\int_{T_{k-1}}^{T_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{2}f_{k}(\tau)\|_{L^{2p}_{x,v}}^{2}d\tau=

\displaystyle\int_{T_{k-1}}^{T_{k}}\|\langle v\rangle^{4}(f_{k}(\tau))^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq(T_{k}-T_{k-1})^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\|\langle v\rangle^{4}(f_{k})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}\leq C_{0}(T_{k}-T_{k-1})^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\frac{2^{k(\frac{\xi_{*}-2p}{2p})}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\frac{r*}{p}}}{K_{0}^{\frac{\xi_{*}-2p}{2p}}}.

Assuming
T\geq 1
, the following analogous estimate holds

\mathcal{E}_{k}\leq C_{l}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{2^{k(a_{i}+1)}\mathcal{E}_{k-1}^{\beta_{i}}}{K_{0}^{a_{i}}},\quad k=1,2,3...,\quad T\geq 1.

Apply the same De Giorgi iteration as Theorem
4.7
we conclude that

\sup_{t\geq T}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{+}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq K_{0}:=K_{0}(\mathcal{E}_{0})=C_{l}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\mathcal{E}_{0}^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}},\quad l\leq k_{0},

where
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{E}_{p,s^{\prime\prime}}(0,T/2,+\infty)
. For any
T\geq 0
and
l\leq l_{0}(k_{0})+k_{0}
, by Lemma
6.3
apply for
f(T)
and Theorem
7.4
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{P}(0,T,+\infty)=

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq T}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{+}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\int_{T}^{+\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\gamma/2}f_{+}\|_{H^{s}_{v}}^{2}dxd\tau

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C_{0}}\left(\int_{T}^{\infty}\|(1-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime\prime}/2}(\langle v\rangle^{l-2+\gamma/2}f_{+})^{2}\|_{L^{p}_{x,v}}^{p}\right)^{1/p}\leq C_{l}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f(T)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2/p}

so the convergence rate follows by the convergence rate in Theorem
7.4
.
∎

8.
Strong singularity

This section is similar as
[
9
]
Section 7. This section we prove the existence of the solution in the case of strong singularity, the only reason that we have to restrict to the weak singularity is because that in the construction of the solution, the regularization term
\epsilon L_{\alpha}
need to be used to control the
H^{2s}
norm, while all the priori estimates is performed for the full range
s\in(0,1)
, we use an additional
\eta
approximation in the collision kernel, recall that the original collision kernel satisfies

b(\cos\theta)\sim\frac{1}{\theta^{2+2s}},\quad s\in(\frac{1}{2},1).

Fix
s_{*}\in(0,\frac{1}{2}]
such that

2s-2s_{*}<1.

For any
\eta\in(0,1)
, let
Q_{\eta}
be the approximate operator with the collision kernel

\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\theta^{2+2s_{*}}}\leq b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)=\frac{b(\cos\theta)\theta^{2+2s}}{\theta^{2+2s^{*}}(\theta+\eta)^{2s-2s_{*}}}\leq b(\cos\theta),

for some constant
\alpha_{0}>0
. We note that the coefficient
\alpha_{0}
is independent of
\eta
since

\frac{1}{(\theta+\eta)^{2s-2s_{*}}}\geq\frac{1}{(\pi+1)^{2s-2s_{*}}},\quad\forall\theta\in(0,\pi),\quad\eta\in(0,1).

For the
Q_{\eta}
term, the upper bound of the collisional kernel does not change, since the proof of Lemma
2.3
only use the upper bound of
f
(which can be seen in
[
39
]
), we still have

Lemma 8.1
.

Lemma
2.3
is still true when
Q
is replaced by
Q_{\eta}
for all
\eta\in[0,1]
, moreover the constant is independent of
\eta
.

In this section, we will consider the modified equation

(56)

\partial_{t}f_{\eta}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\eta}=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f_{\eta})+Q_{\eta}(\mu+f_{\eta}\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{\eta}),\mu+f_{\eta}),\quad f_{\eta}(0)=f_{0},

and its linearized version

(57)

\partial_{t}f_{\eta}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}f_{\eta}=\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f_{\eta})+Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f),\mu+f_{\eta}):=\tilde{Q}_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+f_{\eta}),\quad f_{\eta}(0)=f_{0}.

Choices of weights remain the same as in the previous sections. We will show the details for the basic energy estimates for the linearized equation to illustrate how to derive uniform-in-
\eta
bounds. The rest of the steps are parallel to those before and their details will be either sketched or omitted. The regularization
\epsilon L_{\alpha}
helps to simplify the estimates, since for each fixed
\epsilon
, the gain of velocity regularity (and subsequently the hypoellipticity) now comes from
\epsilon L_{\alpha}
instead of
Q
.

Lemma 8.2
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(0,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
in the cutoff function is small enough such that
G_{\chi}=u+g\chi
satisfies

G_{\chi}\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

for some constant
A<B>0
. If
f_{\eta}
is a solution to (
57
). Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8,\alpha\geq 5
, for any
t\geq 0
we have

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}(t)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{4}\int_{0}^{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2}}^{2}d\tau+\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l,\epsilon}t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+t),

for some constant
c_{0},C_{l}>0
.
If in addition we assume
l\geq 3+2\alpha
, then for any
0<s^{\prime}<1/8
, for any
t\geq 0
we have

\int_{0}^{t}\|(I-\Delta_{x})^{s^{\prime}/2}f_{\eta}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}d\tau\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l,\epsilon}t}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+t),

where the constant
C_{l,\epsilon}
is independent of
\eta
but depends on
\epsilon
while
C_{l}
is independent of
\eta
and
\epsilon
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.8
, the regularization term
\epsilon L_{\alpha}
gives

\epsilon\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}L_{\alpha}(\mu+f_{\eta})f_{\eta}\langle v\rangle^{2l}dvdx\leq-\frac{\epsilon}{2}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\alpha}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l}\epsilon\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}\epsilon\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}.

Since
\epsilon L_{\alpha}
will provide the dominating term in both the weight and the regularity, by Lemma
8.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}),\mu+f_{\eta})f_{\eta}\langle v\rangle^{2l}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}})\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+2s+l+2}\cap L^{2}_{2}}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l+s+\gamma/2}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}}^{2}+\|\langle v\rangle^{l+2s+\gamma}\mu\|_{H^{2s}_{v}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}})dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\|\mu+g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\|\langle v\rangle^{l+s+\gamma/2}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+C_{l}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+s+\gamma/2}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{v}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}+\frac{\epsilon}{4}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\alpha}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2},\quad\alpha\geq 5.

Gathering the two terms we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\alpha}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+\alpha}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+1,

thus the theorem can be proved same way as Lemma
3.9
(taking
s=1
).
∎

The
L^{2}
level set estimate parallel to Lemma
4.1
is

Lemma 8.3
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(\frac{1}{2},1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
in the cutoff function is small enough such that
G_{\chi}=u+g\chi
satisfies

G_{\chi}\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

For some constant
A,B>0
. For any constant
12\leq l\leq k_{0},K\geq 0,\alpha=5
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\epsilon L_{\alpha}(\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon}{4}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}},

for some constant
c_{1},C_{l,\epsilon>}0
, where the constants are all independent of
\eta
.

Proof.

First we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx:=H_{1}+H_{2}.

By Lemma
8.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mu_{*}+g_{*}\chi_{*})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}(f_{k,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}-f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l})b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dvdv_{*}d\sigma dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f_{K,+}^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\mu+g\chi\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma/2+s+l+2}\cap L^{2}_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s}}^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

By term
T_{2},T_{3}
in Lemma
4.1
we have

\displaystyle H_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}(1+\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{2}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)(l+\sup_{x}\|g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{l}})\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{l,\epsilon}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C_{l}(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{2}}.

where the constant
C
is independent of
\eta
, together with Lemma
4.1
(2) the proof is finished.
∎

Similarly as Lemma
4.3
we have

Lemma 8.4
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(\frac{1}{2},1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. For any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
in the cutoff function is small enough such that
G_{\chi}=u+g\chi
satisfies

G_{\chi}\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

For some constant
A,B>0
. Then for any

T\geq 0,\quad\epsilon\in[0,1],\quad j\geq 0,\quad\tau>2,\quad K>0,\quad 12\leq l\leq k_{0},\quad\alpha\geq j+2,

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\left|\langle v\rangle^{j}(1-\Delta_{v})^{-\tau/2}(\tilde{Q}_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,u+f)\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{K,+}^{l})\right|dvdxdt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{j/2}f_{K,+}^{l}(0,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}+C\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C(1+K)\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{1}_{x}L^{1}_{j+2}},

where the constant
C
is independent of
\eta,\epsilon
. Similar estimate holds for
f_{K,+}^{l}
replaced by
h_{K,+}^{l}

Proof.

Recall
W_{k}
is defined in (
29
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+f)f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}dvdx=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}dvdx

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,\mu+\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}dvdx:=H_{1}+H_{2}.

By Lemma
8.1
we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f-\frac{K}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}\langle v\rangle^{l}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mu_{*}+g_{*}\chi_{*})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}(f_{k,+}^{l}(v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime}\rangle^{l}W_{k}(v^{\prime})-f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}(v))b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}dvdv_{*}d\sigma dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(\mu+g\chi,f_{K,+}^{l}\frac{1}{\langle v\rangle^{l}})f_{K,+}^{l}\langle v\rangle^{l}W_{k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\mu+g\chi\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma/2+s+l+2}\cap L^{2}_{2}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}W_{k}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|\mu+g\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{k_{0}}}\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s+j}}^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{s}_{\gamma/2+s+j}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha}f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C\|f_{K,+}^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2}.

The other terms can be proved by following the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Replacing the use of Lemma
3.9
by Lemma
8.2
in the proof
4.6
we have

Lemma 8.5
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(1/2,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
in the cutoff function is small enough such that
G_{\chi}=u+g\chi
satisfies

G_{\chi}\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

for some constant
A<B>0
. If
F_{\eta}=\mu+f_{\eta}
be a solution to (
57
). Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8,\alpha=5,l\geq 3+2\alpha
, for any
T\geq 0,0<s^{\prime}<1/8
, there exist
s"\in 0,(s^{\prime}\frac{4}{2l+r})
and
p^{b}:=p^{b}(l,\gamma,s,s^{\prime})>1
such that for any
1<p<p^{b}
, we have

\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}\leq C_{l}e^{C_{l,\epsilon}T}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\left(\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+T^{j}\right),\quad p^{\prime}=p/(2-p),

where
C_{l,\epsilon}
is independent of
\eta
and
C_{l}
is independent of both
\eta
and
\epsilon
. The same estimates holds for
(-f)_{+}^{l}
and its associated
\mathcal{E}_{0,p,s^{\prime\prime}}
.

Similarly as Theorem
4.7
we have

Theorem 8.6
.

Suppose
\gamma+2s>-1,s\in(1/2,1),\gamma\in(-3,0]
. Fo any smooth function
f,g
, suppose
G=\mu+g\geq 0
and
\delta_{0}>0
in the cutoff function is small enough such that
G_{\chi}=u+g\chi
satisfies

G_{\chi}\geq 0,\quad\inf_{t,x}\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{v}}\geq A,\quad\sup_{t,x}(\|G_{\chi}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}+\|G\|_{L\log L})\leq B,

for some constant
A<B>0
. If
F_{\eta}=\mu+f_{\eta}
be a solution to (
57
). Then for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
,
12\leq l\leq k_{0}-8,\alpha=5,l\geq 3+2\alpha
, for any
T\geq 0
, assume that the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}<+\infty,

there exist a constant
l_{0}>0
which depends on
l
such that if we additionally suppose that

\sup_{t}\|\langle v\rangle^{l+l_{0}}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq C.

Then it follows that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\max\{2\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}},K_{0}^{1}\},

where

K_{0}^{1}:=C_{l}e^{C_{l,\epsilon}T}\max_{1\leq i\leq 4}\max_{j\in\{1/p,p^{\prime}/p\}}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}^{2j}+T^{j})^{\frac{\beta_{i}-1}{a_{i}}},\quad p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{2-p},

where
C_{l,\epsilon}
is independent of
\eta
and
C_{l}
is independent of both
\eta
and
\epsilon
.

It is clear that form Theorem
8.6
, for each
\epsilon>0
if we let
T
be small enough (with smallness depend on
\epsilon
,
\delta_{0}
only) and
\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
small enough (with smallness independent of both
\epsilon
and
\eta
), then

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\langle v\rangle^{l}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{0},

we can now move to the existence of local solutions.

Lemma 8.7
.

Suppose
g,f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(1/2,1),\gamma+2s>-1,\alpha=5
. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
56
). Suppose
k_{0}
and the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies

k_{0}-l_{0}(14)\geq 14+8,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14+l_{0}(14)}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},

where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
8.6
. For any
\delta_{0}>0
small enough, there exist
\delta_{1},\epsilon_{*}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{*}]
, there exist a time
T_{\epsilon}
(independent of
\eta
) such (
49
) has a solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{14}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Moreover the solution
f
satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0}.

Proof.

The proof is similar as Lemma
5.2
. when applying the fixed point argument, the coefficient obtained will depend on
\eta
, specifically, we will have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}Q_{\eta}(g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h),f_{h})(f_{g}-f_{h})\langle v\rangle^{2k}dvdx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\eta^{2s-2s_{*}}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{3}}\|g\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}g)-h\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}h)\|_{L^{1}_{\gamma+2s_{*}+k-\alpha}\cap L^{2}_{2}}\|f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma+2s_{*}+k-\alpha}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{H^{2s_{*}}_{k+\alpha}}dx

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\eta}(\sup_{x}\|f_{h}\|_{L^{2}_{\gamma+2s_{*}+k-\alpha}})\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}\|(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2s_{*}}_{k+\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon}{8}\|\langle v\rangle^{\alpha+k}(f_{g}-f_{h})\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}+C_{\epsilon,\eta}\|g-h\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{k}}^{2},

and other terms can be estimated the same way as Lemma
5.2
. First we can deuce the existence in
T_{\epsilon,\eta}
depends on both
\eta
and
\epsilon
. However, since all the priori estimates are independent of
\eta
, such a solution can be extend to
T_{\epsilon}
which is independent of
\eta
.

∎

Once the existence of
f_{\eta}
is shown, we can pass to the limit and return to the original operator
Q
with
\chi

Lemma 8.8
.

Suppose
g,f
smooth and
\gamma\in(-3,0],s\in(1/2,1),\gamma+2s>-1,\alpha=5
. Assume that
f
is a solution to (
49
). Suppose
k_{0}
and the initial data
f_{0}
satisfies

k_{0}-l_{0}(14)\geq 14+8,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14+l_{0}(14)}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}<+\infty,\quad\mu+f_{0}\geq 0,\quad\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}}\leq\delta_{1},

where
l_{0}
is defined in Theorem
8.6
. For any
\delta_{0}>0
small enough, there exist
\delta_{1},\epsilon_{*}>0
such that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{*}]
, there exist a time
T_{\epsilon}
(independent of
\eta
) such (
49
) has a solution
f\in L^{\infty}([0,T],L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{14}({\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}))
. Moreover the solution
f
satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T_{\epsilon}]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3})}\leq\delta_{0}.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.7
, we know there exist a solution
f_{\eta}
to (
56
) satisfies

\|\langle v\rangle^{14}f_{\eta}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x,v}}\leq\delta_{0},\quad\|f_{\eta}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}_{t,x}H^{1}_{k+\alpha}}\leq C_{0}<+\infty,\quad s^{\prime}<1/8.

Given the uniform polynomial decay and a diagonal argument, we can extract a subsequence, still denote as
f_{\eta}
such that

f_{\eta}\to f,\quad\hbox{strongly in }L^{2}_{t,x,v}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}).

Our goal is to show that

Q_{\eta}(\mu+f_{\eta}\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f_{\eta}),\mu+f_{\eta})\to Q(\mu+f\chi(\langle v\rangle^{k_{0}}f),\mu+f),

is distribution. Using a test function
\phi
we consider the difference

\displaystyle(Q_{\eta}(f_{\eta}\chi_{\eta},f_{\eta})-Q(f\chi,f),\phi)=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}f_{\eta}(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}\chi_{*}f(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}f_{\eta}-f_{*}\chi_{*}f)(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)-b(\cos\theta))|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}\chi_{*}f(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle E_{1}+E_{2}.

For the
E_{1}
term by Lemma
2.15
we have

\displaystyle E_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}f_{\eta}-f_{*}\chi_{*}f)(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}|f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}f_{\eta}-f_{*}\chi_{*}f|\left|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))d\sigma\right|dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}f_{\eta}-f_{*}\chi_{*}f|dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}(|f_{\eta,*}\chi_{\eta,*}-f_{*}\chi_{*}||f_{\eta}|+|f_{*}\chi_{*}||f_{\eta}-f|)dvdv_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}|v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}(|f_{\eta,*}-f_{*}||f_{\eta}|+|f_{*}||f_{\eta}-f|)dvdv_{*},

which implies

\|E_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\leq C\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\|f_{\eta}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{4}}\|f_{\eta}-f\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{4}}\to 0,\quad\eta\to 0.

To estimate
E_{2}
, by symmetry (precisely antisymmetry) and Taylor expansion we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta))|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}\chi_{*}f(\phi(v^{\prime})-\phi(v))dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta))|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}\chi_{*}f(v-v^{\prime})\cdot\nabla_{v}\phi(v)dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta))|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}f_{*}\chi_{*}f(v-v^{\prime})\otimes(v-v^{\prime})\nabla_{v}^{2}\phi(\bar{v})dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\cos\theta)|b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)||v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}|f_{*}\chi_{*}||f||\nabla_{v}\phi(v)|dvdv_{*}dv\sigma\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta|b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)||v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f_{*}\chi_{*}||f||\nabla_{v}^{2}\phi(\bar{v})|dvdv_{*}d\sigma\right|.

The integrands of the two last terms satisfies the uniform in
\eta
bound

(1-\cos\theta)|b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)||v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma}|f_{*}\chi_{*}||f||\nabla_{v}\phi(v)|\leq 2\|\phi\|_{W^{1,\infty}}(1-\cos\theta)b(\cos\theta)|f_{*}||f||v-v_{*}|^{1+\gamma},

and

\sin^{2}\theta|b(\cos\theta)-b_{\eta}(\cos\theta)||v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}|f_{*}\chi_{*}||f||\nabla_{v}^{2}\phi(\bar{v})|\leq 2\|\phi\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\sin^{2}\theta b(\cos\theta)|f_{*}||f||v-v_{*}|^{2+\gamma}.

Since the right hand of the inequalities above are integrable, we can apply the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and obtain that
E_{2}\to 0
as
\eta\to 0
, hence the theorem is proved.

∎

Recall that the only place that the restriction of a weak singularity enters is when we apply the fixed-point argument to obtain an approximate solution to equation. Once such restriction is bypassed, the rest of the results in Section
6
and Section
7
all hold, since they are all proved for
s\in(0,1)
. Theorem
1.2
is thus proved.
